Entry format (§2.2 of the SOP):

```markdown
### <ISO date> — P<n> round <r> — <actor>
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: <executor rounds only: comfortable | tight | ran out — at what point?>
- ACTION:
- COMMITS: <sha…>
- TESTS: <command> → <pass/fail, counts>
- FINDINGS RAISED: <blocking / should-fix / nit>
- DECISIONS:
- BACKLOG ADDED:
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND:
```

### 2026-07-31 — T2 round 1 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — the phase is small in code (8 files, ~525 net lines) but long in wall-clock, because every verification is a full CI cycle. Nothing was cut for context.
- ACTION: Built the CI e2e fixture topology (it did not exist — gate 4 previously built against placeholder env and ran only `smoke.spec.ts` against no Supabase stack). Gate 4 now: `supabase start -x <9 unused services>` → `supabase db reset` (both migrations from scratch) → `.env.local` from `supabase status -o json` → `npm run build` → `node scripts/ci/seed-e2e.mjs` → mandatory spec list → skip guard → `supabase stop --no-backup` (`if: always()`). New: `scripts/ci/e2e-fixtures.json` (shared fixture constants, so seeder and specs cannot drift), `scripts/ci/seed-e2e.mjs` (idempotent admin+docente+school seeder that hard-refuses any non-local Supabase host), `tests/e2e/helpers/auth.ts` (UI login → storageState per fixture), `tests/e2e/ci-fixture.spec.ts` (6 tests), `scripts/ci/e2e-mandatory.mjs` (list + fail-on-skip guard). Playwright config emits a JSON report under CI so the guard has something to read. CLI pinned to 2.110.0 for gate 4 only (it passes service names to `-x`; gate 3 still uses `latest`).
- COMMITS: `decf9e4` (topology), `c6e3c99` (locator fix), `7a95272` (plan docs onto branch), `48c5806` (scratch skip — guard demo), `8ca8c49` (revert of the scratch skip), `c123727` + `42a3a8c` (evidence/review-request/ledger), plus this ledger correction — full list on PR #27
- TESTS: `npx playwright test $(node scripts/ci/e2e-mandatory.mjs --list) --project=chromium` → **8 passed (8.6s)** on run 30602316662 (`smoke.spec.ts` 2 + `ci-fixture.spec.ts` 6). Guard on the real report → `OK — 2 mandatory spec(s) ran with no skips`. Skip demo (run 30602616645) → specs step succeeds, guard step fails, as designed. Gates 1/1b/2/3 green in CI on the branch commit. Seeder verified idempotent (created → reused) and verified to refuse a production URL. Evidence: `docs/plan/evidence/t2/ci-runs.md`.
- FINDINGS RAISED: (should-fix) `pages/login.tsx` has no `data-testid` on its email/password inputs and its labels are not associated with them, so the auth helper must locate the password field by CSS — fix before A6b builds on this helper. (nit) `/admin/schools` renders the same `<h1>` twice (layout header + page heading), which is both an a11y smell and a Playwright strict-mode trap. (process, not code) the repo worktree is shared by concurrent executor sessions: during this phase another session committed its A0 work onto `phase/t2-ci` (preserved at `rescue/a0-6e69c9e`, branch rebuilt to T2-only), ran `supabase test db` against the shared local stack and wiped a seeded fixture set mid-verification, and ran a second concurrent `next build` in the same `.next/`. Recommend one git worktree per concurrent phase.
- DECISIONS: (1) storageState is produced by driving the real login form, not by minting a token — it exercises the auth-helpers cookie chain the middleware actually reads. (2) `beforeAll` + `test.use({storageState})` instead of a Playwright setup project, so no login dependency is attached to the default `chromium` project that ~57 legacy specs use. (3) gate 4 pins the Supabase CLI; gate 3 left on `latest` (out of scope). (4) gates verified in CI rather than locally — the shared worktree carried another phase's uncommitted source, so a local run would have graded a tree that is not this branch.
- BACKLOG ADDED: `data-testid` on the login form inputs (blocks nothing now; wanted before A6b). Duplicate `<h1>` on `/admin/schools`. Gate 3's `supabase db start` + `latest` CLI could adopt the same pinned/`-x` treatment for consistency.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM independent verification, then Codex final review. Later phases add their own specs to `MANDATORY_SPECS` in `scripts/ci/e2e-mandatory.mjs`.

### 2026-07-31 — A0 round 1 — Opus (Executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — small surface (3 source files, 3 test files); the only context cost was gate wall-clock, not reading.
- ACTION: Executor half of A0 ([A2]+[A3]; [A1] Appendix A sign-off is owner work and is NOT claimed). Replaced the clock-derived "Última actualización" line in `components/PrivacyPolicyContent.tsx` with constants — `/privacidad` is statically prerendered, so the served HTML carried the *build* date and hydration replaced it with the *viewer's* local date (hence the `suppressHydrationWarning`, now removed); neither value was citable by a consent record. New `lib/legal/privacy-notice.ts`: `PRIVACY_NOTICE_VERSION='2026-07-v1'`, `PRIVACY_NOTICE_UPDATED_AT='2026-07-30'` (ISO), `PRIVACY_NOTICE_UPDATED_LABEL='30-07-2026'` (written out, not runtime-formatted: a Date from a date-only string is UTC midnight and renders as the prior day in America/Santiago, and `toLocaleDateString` varies with the runtime's ICU build; a test derives dd-mm-yyyy from the ISO string to catch drift), plus `LEGAL_IDENTITY` + `LEGAL_IDENTITY_PENDING` with `taxId`/`streetAddress` holding the visible sentinel `[PENDIENTE: Apéndice A-10]`. New `lib/pasantias/consent.ts`: `CONSENT_PROCESSING_TEXT` (required — respond + deliver the program, links the privacy notice) and `CONSENT_MARKETING_TEXT` (optional — "(Opcional) Quiero recibir novedades…", revocable), textually distinct per D-12, plus `PRIVACY_POLICY_PATH`/`PRIVACY_POLICY_LINK_LABEL` for A6b's link. Tests assert structural properties (distinctness, optionality marker, purpose scoping, sentinel not silently empty, and that the rendered line does not follow a faked system clock) rather than exact prose, so owner rewording under A-14/A-15 will not break the suite.
- COMMITS: `3a90223` (docs-only merge of `docs/comms-plan` onto the phase branch), `82a14cd` (source + tests), plus this ledger/review-request commit. `82a14cd` is a cherry-pick of `6e69c9e`, which this session originally committed onto `phase/t2-ci` by accident — see FINDINGS.
- TESTS: `npx vitest run __tests__/lib/legal/privacy-notice.test.ts __tests__/lib/pasantias/consent.test.ts __tests__/components/PrivacyPolicyContent.version.test.tsx` → 3 files / 16 tests passed, re-run in an isolated worktree on the reconstructed branch. Full gates re-run in that same isolated worktree, all green: `type-check` exit 0; `lint` exit 0 (`--max-warnings=0`); `npm test` → 228 files / 3386 tests passed, exit 0; `npm run build` → `✓ Compiled successfully`, exit 0, `/privacidad` prerendered at 2.44 kB. The earlier run of those four gates was also green but executed in the shared checkout, whose tree carried T2's uncommitted files, so it graded a superset of this branch and is not cited as evidence (the two runs agree: 228/3386). `test:db`/e2e not applicable (no DB or UI-flow change).
- FINDINGS RAISED: **blocking (process, not code) ×1 — two executor sessions shared one working tree, and branch state was corrupted by it.** Concrete sequence: this session cut `phase/a0-content` from `main` and merged the plan docs (`3a90223`); the T2 session then moved the shared checkout's HEAD to a new `phase/t2-ci`; this session's `git commit` therefore landed `6e69c9e` **on T2's branch, not its own** (the commit's parent is `d62d7e9`/main, not `3a90223`); T2 rescued it to `rescue/a0-6e69c9e` and rebuilt their branch, leaving `phase/a0-content` with no source work at all. Recovered by creating an isolated `git worktree` for `phase/a0-content` and cherry-picking the rescued commit (`82a14cd`). Corollaries: neither session's gate evidence was run on its own branch in isolation (both graded a mixed tree); the two ledger entries were appended to two different branch copies of `LEDGER.md` and will conflict when both branches merge; a concurrent `next build` shared one `.next/`. **Parallel phases need one git worktree each — this is not optional, it silently misplaces commits.** **should-fix ×1** — `docs/plan/` is not on `main`, so a phase branch cut from `main` has no `LEDGER.md` to append to; both executors independently merged the docs branch onto their phase branch to work around it, which means merging either phase to `main` also lands the plan docs (drop `3a90223` if unwanted). Land `docs/comms-plan` on `main` first and the workaround disappears. **nit ×1** — the first `npm run build` hung at 0% CPU on an undrained stdout pipe; killed and re-run with file-redirected output. Any exit code from that killed attempt is meaningless.
- DECISIONS: version scheme `2026-07-v1` chosen (plan gave the format as an example) — cheap to change now, expensive after A5 starts stamping it into `consent_notice_version`. `LEGAL_IDENTITY` placed in `lib/legal/` rather than beside the consent constants, because Track B's campaign footer needs the controller identity without importing a pasantías module. Pending A-10 fields render a visible sentinel rather than an empty string; any phase that renders the block must gate on it.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: This entry exists only on `phase/a0-content`; T2's round-1 entry exists only on `phase/t2-ci`. Whoever merges second must reconcile both into one ledger — expect a conflict at the end of the file, and keep both entries. A0 cannot close — [A1] Appendix A sign-off outstanding (A-4, A-5, A-9..A-12, A-14, A-15). Consent prose is a draft pending A-14/A-15. Nothing consumes the constants yet (first consumers: A5 stamps the version, A6b renders the sentences). No `docs/plan/evidence/a0/` created — this phase produces no PDFs/screenshots; test output is the evidence. PM to verify independently, then Brent triggers Codex.

Verified the prompt's premise first: `pages/index.tsx:975-979` submits `inspira|inicia|evoluciona|aula-generativa|otro`, none of which the old `interestMap` had — Inspira leads really did arrive labelled with the raw slug. New map covers all five with es-CL labels and retains `pasantias|consultoria|formacion` as aliases with their original labels. The previously-dead `htmlContent` template is now the body actually sent, via Resend (lazy client, `EMAIL_FROM_ADDRESS` with the house default, soft-fail on missing key) to `info@nuevaeducacion.org`, with `nombre`/`email`/`institucion`/`cargo`/`interes`/`mensaje` all through `escapeHtml` (newlines → `<br>` after escaping). Removed: the Formspree POST, the 50/month 429 block, the `trackFormSubmission` call + its import + the now-unused `createServiceRoleClient` import, and the dead `confirmationHtml`. Added the house best-effort limiter (5/min/IP, identifier `contact`) after the 405 check, matching `lib/signupSubmission.ts` ordering. `lib/formSubmissionTracker.ts`, `pages/index.tsx` and every other file are untouched.
- COMMITS: `5e1940d` (code + tests), plus this docs commit (review request + ledger). Base `3cb3327`.
- TESTS: `npx vitest run __tests__/api/contact.test.ts` → **24 passed**, exit 0. `npm run type-check` → exit 0. `npm run lint` → exit 0. `npm test` → **226 files / 3394 tests passed** (28.81s). `npm run build` → exit 0. `test:db`/`e2e` not run — no DB object or UI surface touched.
- FINDINGS RAISED: (should-fix) a Resend failure still returns 200 with `emailSent:false` — preserved from the Formspree code, but the homepage form persists nothing, so a silent sender outage loses leads outright; the dual-write into `pasantias_leads` is already a backlog item and would close it. (nit) `FORMSPREE_ENDPOINT` may still be set in the Vercel project — now inert, an ops cleanup. (nit) `lib/formSubmissionTracker.ts` + `pages/admin/form-usage.tsx` are now unreferenced by this route; removal stays the standing backlog item.
- DECISIONS: (1) The subject line is plain text and is **not** HTML-escaped — escaping it would put literal `&#39;`/`&amp;` in FNE's inbox for any apostrophe, with no security gain; instead CR/LF are stripped from the three interpolations, with a test. (2) `reply_to: email` added to preserve Formspree's `_replyto` — without it "Reply" would answer `notificaciones@`, which I judged a regression rather than out-of-scope; field is snake_case in resend 3.5.0 (`CreateEmailOptions.reply_to`, verified in the installed `.d.ts`, not from memory). (3) `emailSent` now reports the truth (`false` when nothing was sent) instead of the old `emailSent || !emailError`, which claimed `true` when no transport was configured; response *shape* and all status codes unchanged and the only caller reads neither field. (4) Legacy aliases keep their original labels rather than being remapped onto the new Inspira label, so a legacy payload renders exactly as before. (5) The suite exercises the real `lib/rateLimit` rather than stubbing it (the 429 criterion needs it), isolating cases with per-test synthetic `x-forwarded-for` values.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none new — the two candidates (tracker removal, homepage→leads dual-write) are already on the plan's backlog.

### 2026-07-31 — B1a round 1 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — 10 files, ~660 net lines; the only expensive read was `pages/expense-reports.tsx` (836 lines) and the baseline RLS block for `expense_reports`. Nothing was cut for context.
- ACTION: Migrated both live callers of the unauthenticated `/api/send-email` relay off it. **Finding that shaped the phase:** the prompt's preferred option (extend existing authenticated expense mutation routes) does not exist — there is no `pages/api/expense-reports/**`; the page mutates `expense_reports` straight from the browser under RLS. Took the second option: one narrowly-scoped authed route, `POST /api/expense-reports/[id]/notify`, which sends the notification belonging to the report's **current persisted state**. The browser sends only the id in the path; the request body is never read. The route resolves recipient (`EXPENSE_APPROVER_EMAIL` for submission, owner `profiles.email` for a decision), subject, body, reviewer name (`reviewed_by`) and rejection comments (`review_comments`) from the report row via the service role, and authorizes per moment: `submitted` → only `submitted_by`; `approved`/`rejected` → designated approver or global admin; anything else → 409. Templates moved out of the browser into a new server-only `lib/email/expenseNotifications.ts` (lazy Resend client, `EMAIL_FROM_ADDRESS`, soft-fail without `RESEND_API_KEY`, HTML-escaped interpolation via `lib/utils/html-escape`) — the `pages/api/admin/tractor-signups/grant.ts` pattern. `utils/emailUtils.ts` deleted outright. `lib/bots/expense-service.ts` now calls the sender directly instead of fetching the relay; its now-dead `appUrl` constructor parameter is gone. Best-effort `RATE_LIMITS.expensive` dampening on the new route, because it is replayable by construction. Status mutations were deliberately left client-side (out of B1a's criteria).
- COMMITS: `f29c1ce` (code + tests), plus this docs commit
- TESTS: `npm run type-check` → pass. `npm run lint` → pass (`--max-warnings=0`). `npm test` → **227 files / 3397 tests passed**. `npm run build` → exit 0, route registered as `ƒ /api/expense-reports/[id]/notify`. New: `__tests__/api/expense-reports/notify.test.ts` (16) and `__tests__/lib/email/expenseNotifications.test.ts` (10); `__tests__/lib/bots/expense-service.test.ts` (21) green with mocks moved from fetch-to-relay to the direct sender. Bundle proof in `docs/plan/evidence/b1a/bundle-and-caller-checks.md`: client bundle carries no `api/send-email`, no template strings, no Resend SDK.
- FINDINGS RAISED: (should-fix) the e-mail bodies still hardcode `https://fne-lms.vercel.app/expense-reports`; carried over verbatim because switching to `lib/utils/app-url` changes a production failure mode (`getAppBaseUrl` throws without a configured origin). (should-fix) no expense-report e2e exists to extend; a real end-to-end proof of the three flows needs seeded reports + an approver identity + a mocked Resend boundary, which is phase-sized. (nit) the new endpoint is replayable — a durable fix is a `notified_at` column, not a rate limiter, and that needs a migration.
- DECISIONS: (1) One route deriving the notification moment from stored `status`, rather than three routes or an event parameter — it leaves the client with no input at all beyond the id. (2) Decision-flow authorization is designated-approver **OR** global admin, wider than today's UI gate, matching the DB's `expense_reports_admin_all` write authority; flagged for the reviewer in case parity with the UI is preferred. (3) HTML escaping added while relocating the templates (they interpolated report name / submitter / comments raw) — a deliberate small behaviour change, in the spirit of the phase. (4) Provider error text is logged server-side and not returned to the browser; the response carries only `sent` / `skipped`. (5) `pages/api/test-email.ts` left untouched — it is the single remaining live caller of the relay and B1b deletes both together.
- BACKLOG ADDED: expense-report e2e coverage; `app-url` for expense e-mail deep links (with the production-origin config it requires); durable per-report notification dedup (`notified_at`) if replay is judged a real problem.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM independent verification, then Codex final review. B1b's "no live caller" criterion is now satisfiable — after B1b deletes `send-email.ts` + `test-email.ts` together, nothing references the relay.

### 2026-08-01 — Branch-drift incident + recovery — Fable (PM)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: n/a
- ACTION: **Incident, owned by the PM.** After the T2-era restore, the shared checkout was at some point switched from `main` back to `phase/t2-ci` by another session; the PM's next nine docs commits (064a8a0..8d7b847 — prompt files, A-7 verbatim block, A-8 lodging amendment, D-04 privilege standard, §1.5 rows, record corrections, eight ledger entries) were committed and pushed to that dormant branch while every verification checked FILE STATE but never the REF. Consequences now explained retroactively: the A1-r2 "dispatch race" (the session read the checkout, which was ahead of real main), executors finding prompts in the checkout but not in their origin/main worktrees, and the A2 reconciliation repeatedly seeing a "stale" main that was in fact the real one. Detected when a deterministic-union assert demanded a main-side ledger entry that origin/main provably never had. Recovery: `docs/pm-sync` (PR #33) built from origin/main + the stranded docs' final state — PLAN taken from the t2-ci tip (verified superset; only the two amendment-superseded rows differ), LEDGER via deterministic union (25 merged-phase entries + 8 stranded PM entries = 33, dual-direction no-loss asserts), 8 prompt files. A first cherry-pick attempt was correctly blocked by its own asserts (the corrupted-then-repaired commit pair does not replay) and a pipe-masked failure pushed an empty branch ref — repaired in the same recovery.
- COMMITS: eb73aac + (this entry)
- TESTS: union asserts (dual-direction, zero loss); PLAN/prompts content asserts — all gated pre-commit.
- FINDINGS RAISED: none beyond the incident itself.
- DECISIONS: **New hard rule (binding on the PM): every PM commit block begins with an explicit ref assertion (`test "$(git branch --show-current)" = <expected>`), and no mutating pipeline may mask exit codes (`cmd | tail` never guards a mutation). Dispatches are frozen until the prompts/plan are readable from origin/main.** Post-merge cleanup for Brent's word: reset `origin/phase/t2-ci` to its true tip `3051203` (or delete the merged branch) so the stranded lineage cannot be read as authoritative.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PR #33 merge (Brent); then re-reconcile `phase/a2-leads-db` against true main and hand over the PR #31 merge line; then flip the shared checkout to main (no live sessions) and resume dispatches.

### 2026-07-31 — A2 round 1 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — 2 SQL files + docs, ~505 new lines; the only large reads were the `tractor_signups` slices of the 26k-line baseline and `020-tractor-signups-rls.sql`. Nothing was cut for context.
- ACTION: Created `public.pasantias_leads` in one additive migration (`20260731140500_add_pasantias_leads.sql`) with the column set specced in A2 [A1], four CHECKs (email normalization, `num_people` 1–60, status set, marketing all-or-nothing), `UNIQUE (email_normalized, cohort)`, indexes on `(status)` and `(created_at DESC)`, and the shared `set_updated_at` trigger. [A2]: RLS enabled with **exactly one** policy — `pasantias_leads_admin_select`, `FOR SELECT TO authenticated`, `USING` the same `user_roles` admin-EXISTS test as `tractor_signups_admin_all`; **no `WITH CHECK` exists anywhere on the table**, which is what makes the D-04 posture per-operation rather than per-table. [A3]: nothing dropped, truncated or destructively altered; the migration header carries the full access matrix and the forward-only rollback rule, and the policy is created inside a `DO … IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_policies …)` block rather than `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` + `CREATE`, so it is re-runnable without a DROP. `CREATE OR REPLACE TRIGGER` for the same reason. [A4]: `supabase/tests/030-pasantias-leads-rls.sql`, 21 asserts. Status transitions are deliberately **not** enforced in SQL (D-03: the graph lives in `lib/pasantias/leads.ts`, and is authoritative only because D-04 leaves no authenticated write path).
- COMMITS: `c2ceb35` (migration + pgTAP + evidence), plus this docs commit
- TESTS: `npm run test:db` → **Files=5, Tests=47, Result: PASS** (the A2 file: 21/21). Run after `supabase db reset`, so every migration including this one was applied from scratch in order — not hand-patched onto the live local schema. `npm run type-check` → clean. `npm run lint` → clean (`--max-warnings=0`). `npm test` → **232 files / 3445 tests passed**. `npm run build` → succeeded. Per-assert TAP output committed at `docs/plan/evidence/a2/030-pasantias-leads-rls.tap.txt`.
- FINDINGS RAISED: none blocking. Codex scrutiny items, all written up in the review-request: (1) the `DO`-block policy creation would silently leave a same-named pre-existing policy in place — impossible on a new table, but it is a judgment call made to honour the no-DROP hard rule; (2) grants are inherited from Supabase's default privileges and not stated in SQL (the `tractor_signups` posture) — the suite proves the effect, not the grant set; (3) `service_role` is impersonated via `set_config('role','service_role')` relying on `BYPASSRLS` (verified `rolbypassrls = t`), which is a stand-in for the real service-role JWT path.
- DECISIONS: (1) `[A2]`'s negative claim ("no other policies") is asserted directly off `pg_policies` — one aggregate of `policyname|cmd|roles`, plus a count of policies with non-NULL `with_check` — rather than pgTAP's `policies_are`/`policy_cmd_is`, to avoid depending on the installed pgTAP version. (2) Assert count is 21, not the prompt's ~16: added the two structural policy asserts, `consent_notice_version` NOT NULL (the criterion names the consent columns plural), docente DELETE, and a `set_updated_at` trigger check — the trigger is in the [A1] spec and nothing else in the suite would catch its absence. No assert from the prompt's list was dropped. (3) `UNIQUE` shipped as a named table constraint rather than a `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX` (baseline style) so the 23505 carries the constraint name the A5 dedup path will branch on.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none. Flagged for the plan, not the backlog: `num_people`'s upper bound of 60 has no recorded product rationale — implemented as specced, not invented.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM independent verification, then Codex final review. A5 (lead API) is unblocked on the schema side once A4 lands; A5 must not introduce any authenticated write path, or D-03's app-layer transition guard stops being authoritative.

### 2026-07-31 — A2 round r2 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — two SQL files plus docs; the only sizeable reads were REVIEW-A2.md, the round-r1 migration/suite, and the baseline's `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` block. Nothing was cut for context.
- ACTION: Remediated both BLOCKING findings in the same two files, in place (no second migration). **[B1]** Reproduced the finding first against the local stack — the baseline's `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE postgres IN SCHEMA public GRANT ALL ON TABLES TO anon/authenticated` gave the new table `DELETE,INSERT,REFERENCES,SELECT,TRIGGER,TRUNCATE,UPDATE` for both roles, and a rollback-contained `SET ROLE anon; TRUNCATE …` succeeded. The migration now carries, after the policy block, `REVOKE ALL … FROM anon` and `REVOKE INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE, REFERENCES, TRIGGER … FROM authenticated`; `authenticated` keeps SELECT (RLS-gated), `service_role` untouched. Post-reset catalog: `anon` has no grant row at all, `authenticated` has exactly `SELECT`. The header access matrix is rewritten as **two layers** (GRANTs decide which commands a role may attempt; the policy decides which rows the surviving SELECT returns) instead of presenting RLS as the whole boundary; the table COMMENT says the same in one clause. **[B2]** anon now has the full per-operation matrix — SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE — and `authenticated` gains a TRUNCATE denial (the exact probe Codex ran).
- COMMITS: `ad27bcc` (migration + pgTAP + refreshed evidence + review-request round-r2 section), plus this docs commit
- TESTS: `supabase db reset` then `npm run test:db` → **Files=5, Tests=56, Result: PASS** (the A2 file: **30/30**, was 21/21). `npm run type-check` → clean. `npm run lint` → clean (`--max-warnings=0`). `npm test` → **232 files / 3445 tests passed**. `npm run build` → succeeded. The four JS gates cannot be affected by a diff confined to two `.sql` files; run anyway and recorded. Refreshed per-assert TAP at `docs/plan/evidence/a2/030-pasantias-leads-rls.tap.txt`.
- FINDINGS RAISED: none blocking. One consequence the reviewer must look at: **eight pre-existing asserts changed their expected outcome**, unavoidably. PostgreSQL checks table privileges *before* RLS, so once the grant is gone the command never reaches the policy — INSERT (admin/docente/anon) now raises `42501 permission denied for table` instead of `42501 new row violates row-level security policy`, and UPDATE/DELETE (admin/docente) plus anon SELECT now raise instead of returning empty. The repo's "blocked UPDATE returns empty" convention describes an *RLS* denial and does not survive a privilege denial; the suite header now states this explicitly.
- DECISIONS: (1) The prompt's [B2] wording asked for "anon UPDATE matches-0 / DELETE matches-0". After [B1]'s revocation that outcome is unreachable — anon cannot match 0 rows because it cannot execute the statement — and an `is_empty` on a raising statement would abort the suite's transaction rather than fail one assert. Implemented as `throws_ok '42501'`, which is REVIEW-A2.md's own closure wording ("the repository's correct blocked operation semantics") and a strictly stronger claim. (2) Plan 21 → 30: five catalog asserts (exact grant set per role for anon/authenticated, explicit `has_table_privilege(...,'TRUNCATE')` false for both, and `service_role` CRUD intact) + four new behavioral denials (admin TRUNCATE, anon UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE). (3) `authenticated`'s revoke is a **denylist** of the six non-SELECT privileges rather than revoke-all-then-`GRANT SELECT`; the two exact-grant-set asserts pin the resulting state, so a future new PostgreSQL table privilege would fail the suite rather than leak in silently — but B3's five tables should copy whichever form Codex prefers, and this is the round to say so.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM verification, then Codex round 2 (cap 2). Two forward consequences: **anon can no longer SELECT this table at all**, so A5 must not assume a readable-but-empty table via the anon key; and the same REVOKE + privilege-assert pattern now binds B3's five email tables per the amended D-04.

Codex binds B3 to the revoke-all-then-GRANT-SELECT form (resolving the flagged form question). **PM fact-check with own hands: production runs PostgreSQL 15.8 — `MAINTAIN` does not exist there; the finding is forward-looking (materializes on a future PG17 upgrade or for tables created under PG17 defaults), not a live exposure.** Also explains the executor's miss: their grant-set pin used the SQL-standard catalog view, which never surfaces PG-specific privileges. ### 2026-08-01 — A2 round r3 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable for the code; the round's cost was environment recovery, not reasoning. The two SQL files, the r3 prompt, the r2 review-request section and the evidence file were the only substantial reads.
- ACTION: Closed REVIEW-A2-R2.md's remaining finding. **Migration**: `authenticated`'s enumerated REVOKE replaced with the grant-list form — `REVOKE ALL … FROM authenticated;` then `GRANT SELECT … TO authenticated;` (`REVOKE ALL … FROM anon` unchanged); the header LAYER-1 matrix now states that the grant-list form is the standard precisely because a privilege that does not exist yet can never leak into it, and names `MAINTAIN` as the case in point. **Suite**: asserts 4–5 moved off `information_schema.role_table_grants` — a SQL-standard view that reports only standard privileges, which is exactly how r2's pin missed `MAINTAIN` — onto `aclexplode(pg_class.relacl)`, so the pins now cover every privilege the server defines, present or future. Two new version-guarded `MAINTAIN` asserts added (plan 30 → 32), the privilege call wrapped in `pg_temp.lacks_maintain` so the untaken branch is structurally never evaluated rather than relying on the planner not folding a STABLE function.
- COMMITS: (this commit)
- TESTS: `npm run test:db` → **Files=5, Tests=58, Result: PASS** (030 file: **32/32**, was 30/30). `npm run type-check` → clean. `npm run lint` → clean (`--max-warnings=0`). `npm test` → **232 files / 3445 tests passed**. `npm run build` → succeeded. Refreshed TAP + live ACL dump + sub-PG17 branch proof at `docs/plan/evidence/a2/030-pasantias-leads-rls.tap.txt`.
- FINDINGS RAISED: **The r3 prompt's PG15 premise is wrong for local and CI.** The prompt states as PM-verified fact that the local stack is PostgreSQL 15 and cannot exercise a PG17-only privilege. Local and CI are **PostgreSQL 17.6** — `supabase/config.toml` pins no `[db] major_version`, so the CLI's default image applies, and CI gate 3 uses the same unpinned `supabase/setup-cli@v1`. Only the hosted production DB is 15.8 (that half of the PM's check was against production and is correct). Consequences: (1) the acceptance criterion's "MAINTAIN asserts skipping visibly" does not happen — they run **live** and pass, which is stronger evidence, so the criterion is met in substance but not in the letter; (2) the PM triage's "forward-looking, not a live exposure" rationale is half wrong — tables created on the PG17 local/CI stacks carry `MAINTAIN` in their default privileges **today** (visible in the committed ACL dump); production at 15.8 is still unaffected, so the severity call stands but the reasoning behind it should be corrected in the record; (3) the version guard is still required, for the opposite direction — so the suite can run against the 15.8 production server. Skip-branch rendering and guard laziness were proven separately with a scratch script (output committed in the evidence file, script not committed).
- DECISIONS: (1) Plan 30 → 32 rather than folding MAINTAIN into the existing pins — the ACL pins already cover it implicitly, but an explicitly named assert is what a reader greps for. (2) Kept the r2 `has_table_privilege(..., 'TRUNCATE')` asserts alongside the new ACL pins: they are per-privilege behavioral probes, not grant-set pins, and the prompt scoped the replacement to the pins. (3) Fixed two comments that would have shipped the prompt's incorrect PG15 claim into the repo.
- ENVIRONMENT (disclosed because it can manufacture a false green): `supabase db reset` and `supabase start` both wedged on this machine — the CLI sat idle in `ClientRead` at "Initialising schema...", the Docker daemon went unresponsive once and needed a restart, and the CLI's image pulls then stalled indefinitely. The schema under test was built by applying `supabase/migrations/*.sql` in order with `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1` onto a freshly initialised container — same files, same order, from scratch. Two repairs were then needed: (a) `auth.uid()` ships in the Postgres image in its pre-2021 `request.jwt.claim.sub` form and is modernised by GoTrue's migration `20211202183645_update_auth_uid.up.sql`, which never ran because the auth image could not be pulled — **suites 010 and 020, untouched by this round, failed too**, which is how the cause was identified; that migration's SQL was extracted from the GoTrue image and applied verbatim. (b) `public.schema_migrations` (the `pop` migrator's tracking table, `varchar(14)`) was debris from an attempted `gotrue migrate` and broke `001-rls-enabled.sql`; dropped from the local throwaway container. Nothing in the repository, the migrations, the test files, or any non-local database was touched. **CI gate 3 is the authoritative execution** and runs the same PG17 image.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM verification, then the scoped Codex confirmation on Brent's §1.5 authority. Two record corrections for the PM: the local/CI PostgreSQL version, and the "no server we run has MAINTAIN" half of the r2 triage rationale. Forward note for B3 unchanged: its five tables take the grant-list form and should pin privileges via `aclexplode`, not `information_schema`.

### 2026-08-02 — B1b round 3 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — one test file, one finding, no ambiguity; nothing near a limit.
- ACTION: Closed REVIEW-B1B-R2.md's single BLOCKING [B1] (owner-authorized r3 per §1.5). `__tests__/utils/no-browser-mail-transport.test.ts` no longer hand-picks its client-source universe: `CLIENT_SURFACES` is derived at run time as every top-level directory minus dot-directories, minus the plain-name roots parsed from `tsconfig.json`'s own `exclude`, minus `NON_CLIENT_ROOTS` (`lib`, `scripts`, `supabase`, `tests`, `docs` — each carrying its reason in code). Result: `components config constants contexts hooks pages public src styles types utils` — **546 client files, up from 500**, with `src/` (the omission Codex found) now covered. The repo-wide sweep's universe became every compiled root (1124 files) instead of `clientFiles + lib + scripts`, so its "nothing anywhere invokes the edge function" claim is now true of what it reads. Non-vacuity test extended per Codex's §1.5 note: asserts `src` ∈ `CLIENT_SURFACES` and that `src/components/TipTapEditor.tsx` + `components/meetings/MeetingDocumentationModal.tsx` are in the swept list, on top of the >100-file floor. No runtime code touched.
- COMMITS: `07ae6b1` (guard) + this docs commit
- TESTS: `npm run type-check` → pass; `npm run lint` → pass (`--max-warnings=0`); `npm test` → **233 files / 3448 tests passed** (unchanged from r2 — an existing file was edited, no test added); `npm run build` → exit 0. **Bite proof:** injecting `supabase.functions.invoke('send-email', { body: { to, subject, html } })` into `src/components/TipTapEditor.tsx` fails **both** transport assertions (verbatim output in evidence §7); the round-2 collector run against the same injected file reports `offenders: []` and `src/components/TipTapEditor.tsx swept? false`. Reverted, re-run green.
- FINDINGS RAISED: none. No mail transport surfaced in any newly-swept root (`src`, `config`, `constants`, `types`, `styles`, `public`) — the extension closes a hole, it did not expose a live one.
- DECISIONS: (1) **Derived over enumerated** — the fix is the direction of the default: a directory is swept unless someone writes down why not, so the next `src`-shaped omission cannot happen by forgetting. (2) `public/` **is** swept, though nothing bundles it: `public/sw.js` is browser-executed, same capability class. Flagged for the reviewer as the one arguable inclusion. (3) `lib/` stays excluded from the client sweep (unchanged from r2, Codex-accepted): it is the legitimate home of the server senders, and client surfaces are instead forbidden from importing `lib/emailService` / `lib/email/`. (4) The tsconfig parse deliberately reads only glob-free, extension-free `exclude` entries, so a future `"src/legacy/**"` exclusion would leave that tree swept — fail-closed, documented in the review request. (5) Scope held to the prompt: one test file + evidence, no source change, no re-litigation of the transport-keyed / source-level choices Codex accepted.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none. Round-2 deferrals stand (server-side rebuild of task-assignment notification; comment-only SendGrid references and the unauthenticated service-role posture in `pages/api/cron/email-digest.ts`).
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM verification, then the scoped Codex confirmation Brent authorized under §1.5. Branch `phase/b1b-relay` pushed; no merge without Brent's explicit go. B2 unblocks on B1b DONE.

### 2026-08-03 — A1 round r6 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — a three-file micro-round; the only long steps were `npm install` in a fresh worktree and the production build.
- ACTION: Applied the owner's 2026-08-02 headline decision. `buildCohortDateLabel` now flattens the weeks' **visit days** and spans first→last instead of joining two block ranges with `" y "`: `Octubre, 5 al 16`, month named once at the front, still fully derived (no literal date string). `COHORT_HEADLINE` could not stay `${COHORT_LABEL} · ${COHORT_DATE_LABEL}` — `COHORT_LABEL` is "Octubre 2026" and the new span already names the month, so that composition would have rendered **"Octubre 2026 · Octubre, 5 al 16"**. It is now `${COHORT_DATE_LABEL} · ${COHORT_YEAR}` with `COHORT_YEAR` read off `COHORT_WEEKS[0].startDate` (module-private, no new public export), so the card renders **`Octubre, 5 al 16 · 2026`** — the exact span, 2026 still visible, month printed once. `COHORT_LABEL` untouched (Appendix A-1 cohort label; `cohort-commercial.ts` builds the price-validity line from it) and `pages/index.tsx` untouched (it already renders the constant). Tests: the two old pins replaced, plus a prohibition test (no en dash, no `" y "`, no `2026` inside the span, exactly one `2026` and one `Octubre` in the headline) and a derivation test (a synthetic November calendar → `Noviembre, 3 al 11`, which no hardcoded string can pass); the leak-guard suite's "public headline" negative control re-cut from the new string so it still tests the real headline. Rendered proof (prerendered `index.html`, old string absent from `.next`, TZ sweep, leak scanner) at `docs/plan/evidence/a1/headline-span.md`; review request at `docs/planning/reviews/fase-a1r6-review-request.md`.
- COMMITS: `948e616` (code + tests + evidence + review request) + this ledger commit
- TESTS: targeted `npx vitest run __tests__/lib/pasantias-cohort.test.ts __tests__/scripts/check-price-leak.test.ts` → 54/54 (38 + 16; cohort file was 36). TZ sweep UTC / Europe/Madrid / America/Santiago → 38/38 each. Gates: `npm run type-check` clean · `npm run lint` clean (0 warnings) · `npm test` → **235 files / 3502 tests passed** · `npm run build` succeeded · `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → OK, 266 files scanned, exit 0.
- FINDINGS RAISED: **(should-fix, PM-owned)** `PLAN.md:125` — Phase A1 criterion [A4] still pins the retired string `"Octubre 2026 · 5–9 y 13–16 de octubre"`. The 2026-08-02 amendment reached Appendix A-1 and the Decision Log but not the phase criteria, so A1's own acceptance line now contradicts the owner's decision. PLAN.md is the PM's file; not touched here. (Same class of gap as the A-8 propagation miss found in A1 r3.)
- DECISIONS: (1) **Headline recomposed rather than left duplicating the month** — the prompt's premise that the year "already renders separately (card title)" is not true of this card: the year lived only inside `COHORT_HEADLINE`, in the same `<p>`. Kept it in that element, moved it behind the span. The alternative that follows the prompt literally ships "Octubre" twice, which is exactly the kind of headline the owner just rejected. (2) **Span ends taken from `visitDays`, not from the week blocks** — the prompt said so, and for this cohort the two are indistinguishable (both 5 and 16); the synthetic-November test is what tells them apart. (3) **`COHORT_YEAR` kept module-private** — nothing downstream needs it, and every new public export widens the D-01 namespace guard's surface for no gain. (4) **Evidence committed under `docs/plan/evidence/a1/`** rather than only in the report, per META — the rendered card is the acceptance criterion and a constant-pin test does not prove it (Codex S2 remains the durable fix).
- BACKLOG ADDED: none (S1/S2 carried unchanged)
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM verification + the fast Sol nod, then the owner's word to merge `phase/a1-r6-label` independently. **Before that merge, PLAN.md [A4] needs the amended string** (finding above). A3 and A6a inherit the new span through the module — no change needed on their side.

### 2026-08-03 — B2 round 1 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — the phase is read-heavy (two SDK bundles, four Vercel doc pages) but writes little; ended with room to spare.
- ACTION: Compatibility spike, no feature code. **Resend 3.5.0** read from the shipped bundle and locked in `__tests__/lib/resend-contract.test.ts` (13 tests, real `Resend` + stubbed `globalThis.fetch`): `POST /emails/batch` takes the **bare array**; the response double-nests to `data.data[i].id` (plan's suspicion CONFIRMED); per-email `headers` survive serialisation, so D-08's per-recipient `List-Unsubscribe` pair is mechanically possible; **errors are always resolved values, never thrown** — the sole throw in the SDK is the constructor on a missing key, so a `try/catch`-only drain would see nothing. Two reality-vs-type gaps locked: `ErrorResponse` is declared `{name,message}` but the SDK returns the API's JSON verbatim (a real 422 carries `statusCode`), and an `application_error`/"Unable to fetch data" result is an **unknown** outcome — the same value is returned for a network failure and for a 200 with an unparseable body, which is a genuine duplicate-send path independent of cron. **Idempotency: absent in 3.5.0** — the only request option is `PostOptions.query`, which POSTs ignore (spread into the fetch init, where `fetch` drops it); the API *does* support `Idempotency-Key` and current SDKs expose `idempotencyKey`, but 3.5.0 has no way to reach it, and the obvious workaround is pinned as a footgun test: passing `{headers}` through request options **replaces the SDK's Headers wholesale and sends the request unauthenticated**. **svix 1.99.1** added as a dependency and locked in `__tests__/lib/svix-contract.test.ts` (22 tests, real HMACs + fake timers): pinned known-answer vector; tolerance is `5*60` s checked **symmetrically** — ±299 s accepted, ±301 s rejected, so D-08's "past AND future" needs no extra code in B7; multi-signature is space-separated with non-`v1` entries skipped and any matching `v1` accepted (secret rotation survivable); the signature covers **raw bytes** (a `JSON.parse`→`stringify` round-trip fails), so B7 needs `bodyParser: false`; and the two non-`WebhookVerificationError` throws — empty secret (plain `Error`) and signed-but-unparseable body (`SyntaxError`) — are asserted, because collapsing them into a 401 would tell Resend "rejected, don't retry" on every event of a misconfigured deploy. **Findings file** `docs/plan/reviews/fase-b2-findings.md` (378 lines) with a 15-row contract card (C1–C15) for B3/B4/B7/B8/B10 to cite, six open risks, and the mutation evidence.
- COMMITS: (this commit) on `phase/b2-spike` from `origin/main` `2613b46`
- TESTS: `npm run type-check` exit 0; `npm run lint` exit 0 (`--max-warnings=0`); `npm test` → **237 files / 3535 tests passed** (+2 files / +35); `npm run build` exit 0; `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → OK, 266 files; `npx playwright test tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts` → 2 passed (CI gate 4's mandatory spec). **`test:db` NOT run** — zero SQL in this phase, and the only live Supabase container is a parallel worktree's `supabase_db_*` with all other services stopped; not worth disturbing A3 to prove something B2 doesn't touch. Both new suites **mutation-tested**: un-nesting the batch response, switching the SDK to thrown errors, and widening the svix tolerance to 10 min turned exactly 5 tests red (5 failed / 30 passed), restored from pre-mutation backups → 35 passed. Recorded trap: mutating `resend/dist/index.js` reads false-green because vitest resolves the ESM `index.mjs`.
- FINDINGS RAISED: **none blocking — the FINDINGS gate did NOT fire.** Vercel docs (2026-06-16): Hobby = once-per-day minimum with ±59 min jitter and sub-daily expressions **fail deployment**; Pro/Enterprise = once per minute, per-minute precision. This project is **Pro**, on the owner's own in-repo record — `docs/planning/zoom-integration-plan.md:134`, "Vercel Pro confirmation RESOLVED 2026-07-29", closing what that workstream had listed as a blocking owner decision for per-minute crons. The Hobby-era commits (`94a66a8` downgrading `*/5 * * * *` → daily; `4616035` disabling crons) predate it and explain today's empty `crons`. So **D-07's per-minute invoker stands and B10a is not blocked**. Honest residue (R1): I could not re-verify the tier myself — `vercel whoami` → "No existing credentials found", no `VERCEL_TOKEN`, Vercel MCP needs an OAuth flow unavailable here, deploying is forbidden — so this rests on a five-day-old second-hand record. `vercel login && vercel teams ls` closes it, and a stale assumption would fail B10a's *deployment* rather than misbehave silently. Also VERIFIED for B10a: cron is a **GET to production**, `CRON_SECRET` auto-sent as `Authorization: Bearer` (D-07 already correct), **never retried**, **may be missed, may double-fire, and overlapping ticks are not prevented** — D-07's `SKIP LOCKED` claims and its no-`pending`-and-no-`sending` completion predicate already survive all three; only the *causes* listed beside its "≤1 batch" tradeoff need widening. One wire-format note for B7: Resend event names are dotted (`email.sent`, `email.bounced`), while D-08's table uses shorthand — read as prose shorthand, not a design gap, since unknown types already fall to a ledger-only 200.
- DECISIONS: (1) **No SDK upgrade** (3.5.0 → 6.18.1 is three majors and would invalidate every contract locked here plus re-open B1a/B1b's send paths); the idempotency choice is written up as three costed options with a recommendation to stay put, and left to the PM. (2) Tests mock the **transport**, never the library — real `Resend`/`Webhook` classes throughout, so an upgrade breaks the suites loudly. (3) Two hazard-locking tests kept deliberately (the header-spread footgun, the two non-verification throws) because B10a and B7 are the phases most likely to walk into them. (4) `vercel.json` left untouched — the cron entry is B10a's.
- BACKLOG ADDED: R2 revisit the Resend SDK upgrade post-v1 (would bring first-party webhook verification and likely retire `svix`). R4 **B11b must read `List-Unsubscribe` off a genuinely received message** — the wire format is locked but Resend honouring per-email headers on a batch send is verified from docs only, and D-08 compliance depends on it. R3 B10a should assert `data.data.length === payload.length` before zipping provider ids.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM verification, then Codex review. Branch `phase/b2-spike` pushed; no merge without Brent's explicit go. B3 (email schema) and B8 (renderer) unblock on B2 DONE; B10a additionally needs B4a. Brent's one-line confirmation of the Vercel tier (R1) is worth collecting before B10a's `vercel.json` entry ships.

### 2026-08-03 — B2 round r2 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — the round is read-heavy (seven SDK tarballs unpacked and diffed, one library bundle mutated seven times) but the write surface is one test file and two docs; ended with room.
- ACTION: Closed Sol's findings 1 and 2. **Step 1 was the main reconciliation** (PR #36 was CONFLICTING with no CI): `docs/plan/PLAN.md` taken from main verbatim (zero diff asserted), `docs/plan/LEDGER.md` unioned with a script that asserted every `### ` heading of both parents survives — branch parent 73, main parent 77, union 80, final 80, nothing missing, nothing invented. **[F2] The svix suite now kills its own mutants.** r1's "raw bytes" case round-tripped a *subtree* (`JSON.parse(PAYLOAD).data`), so it only proved a different **value** is rejected — which is exactly why Sol's canonicalising verifier stayed green. Replaced with a table of four **value-identical, byte-different** spellings (pretty-printed / space-separated / key-order swapped / trailing newline), each asserting `toEqual` the signed value **and** `not.toBe` its bytes before the rejection, plus a reverse-direction case (sign pretty, verify compact) that kills a both-sides canonicaliser; the old subtree case kept under an honest name. Tolerance re-derived at the **exact** boundary: ±300 accepted (the library compares with `>`, so the window is inclusive) and ±301 rejected, both sides. 22 → 29 tests. **Own mutation set run: 7 mutants, 7 killed** — SM1 tolerance constant 5·60→10·60 (2 killed), SM2 both `>`→`>=` (2 — Sol's surviving boundary mutant), SM3 verify-side canonicalisation (4 — Sol's other surviving mutant), SM4 both-sides canonicalisation (4), SM5 version filter dropped (1), SM6 signature comparison inverted (18), SM7 sorted-key canonicalisation (6, and the only mutant the key-order spelling fires on — a plain `JSON.stringify(JSON.parse(x))` preserves member order). One at a time, from a byte-for-byte backup, each anchor asserted unique, SHA-256 restore assert between mutants; verbatim output + driver committed at `docs/plan/evidence/b2/svix-mutation.md`. **[F1] Idempotency section rebuilt on arithmetic** (§1.4.1–§1.4.4). Exposure per failure mode against the **amended** D-07 bound (≤2 campaigns × ≤3 claims × ≤200 rows, ≤100/batch ⇒ 12 provider calls / 1 200 recipients per tick): **M1** crash between a returned `batch.send` and its per-batch ledger write = **≤100 recipients** (the plan's existing "≤1 batch", correct for this mode only); **M2** `application_error` ambiguity, independent per call = **≤1 200 per tick and repeatable per retry round** — 12× what r1 quoted; **M3** duplicate/overlapping cron = **0**, because `SKIP LOCKED` gives the concurrent tick a disjoint claim (r1 listed it as a duplication path; it is not). Also corrected §1.5's "≤3 calls, ~3 orders of magnitude of headroom, no pacing needed" to the real 12 calls against a **team-wide** 10 req/s ceiling shared with `contact.ts`/expense/tractor sends, citing the amended D-07 pacing rule; §3.2 R1 **closed** on the owner's first-hand Pro confirmation (residue and the `vercel login` step removed); §3.4's wrong duplicate-cron sentence replaced; C16 (pacing + real worst case) and C17 (a 429 is a *definite* failure, never `application_error`) added to the contract card; R5 re-graded from "low, bounded" to "medium, unbounded across retries".

Head `ebf0ba6`; `origin/main` is an ancestor, PR #36 now MERGEABLE.
- TESTS: `npx vitest run __tests__/lib/{resend,svix}-contract.test.ts` → **42 passed** (13 + 29, was 35). Gates: `npm run type-check` exit 0 · `npm run lint` exit 0 (`--max-warnings=0`) · `npm test` → **237 files / 3544 tests passed** (was 3535; +9 svix) · `npm run build` exit 0 · `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → OK, 266 files. Mutation run: baseline 29/29 → 7 mutants each red → restored 29/29. `test:db` not run (zero SQL in this phase, as r1).
- FINDINGS RAISED: **The minimum Resend version is 4.5.0, not the 4.3.0 REVIEW-B2.md names.** Read from the published tarballs' shipped `dist/`, stable releases only: 3.5.0 (last stable 3.x — 3.6.0 never left canary) through **4.2.0** contain zero occurrences of `idempotencyKey`; **4.3.0** introduces `IdempotentRequest` but wires it only to `CreateEmailRequestOptions` and `Resend.post()` — `CreateBatchRequestOptions extends PostOptions {}` is **empty**, so `batch.send(p, { idempotencyKey })` does not type-check, and 4.3.0's `post()` does `this.headers.set("Idempotency-Key", …)` on the **client-level** Headers, so the key would persist onto every subsequent POST from that client (a drain issuing 12 calls per tick from one client would send batches 2…12 under batch 1's key — silently dropped sends). 4.4.1 fixes the leak (per-request header copy); **4.5.0** is the first release where the batch options type carries it. So the minimum is **one major**, not three. Upgrade delta for this repo, read from the tarballs: `CreateBatchSuccessResponse`/`CreateBatchResponse`, `ErrorResponse` and the whole of `fetchRequest`'s error mapping are **byte-identical** in 4.5.2 (C2/C4/C5 survive untouched); exactly one source line breaks and `tsc` catches it (`reply_to`→`replyTo`, `pages/api/contact.ts:170`); two test re-cuts (the `resend-node:3.5.0` UA pin, the three absence assertions); transitive `@react-email/render` 0.0.16 → 1.1.2 whose peers `react ^18.0 || ^19.0` this repo satisfies at 18.3.1. **Second finding, for the PM:** all three non-status-quo options need a column B3 does not define — a persisted per-batch idempotency key for (b)/(c), a non-auto-retriable `unknown` outcome for (d). D-10's additive rule means the decision *can* still be made at B10a, but B3 is the cheap moment. **Third, minor:** B10a's [A3] does not name a 429 test; the card now requires one (C17).
- DECISIONS: (1) **No recommendation offered** in §1.4.4, deliberately — r1's "(a) for now" rested on the three-major figure that §1.4.2 disproves, and the choice is the PM's at the B10a gate. Four options are costed instead of three: Sol's required "genuine no-idempotency safety choice" is written up as (d), a non-auto-retriable `unknown` outcome with operator reconciliation. (2) **"0 duplicates" for the keyed options is stated as conditional**, not absolute — it holds within Resend's 24 h key window and only if the retried request has the same composition, which implies stamping the key on the send rows before the provider call. Claiming it unconditionally would have re-made r1's mistake in the other direction. (3) **SM7 added beyond the prompt's four required mutants** because without a sorting canonicaliser the key-order spelling never fires, and a test case no mutant can kill is decoration. (4) Ledger union resolved main-side-first (main's ordering kept verbatim, branch's B2 entries appended) rather than interleaved by date — interleaving would have rewritten main's lines for no gain.
- BACKLOG ADDED: **[S1] carried, not fixed** — §6.1's second resend mutation is still labelled "an SDK that switches to thrown errors" when it demonstrates altered error *mapping* (Resend's own catch blocks swallow the throw). SHOULD-FIX in REVIEW-B2.md; the r2 prompt scoped this round to findings 1 and 2, so it stays here. A separate mutant that rethrows from the outer `fetchRequest` catch would prove the resolved-vs-rejected contract properly.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM verification, then Codex round 2 (of max 2). Branch `phase/b2-spike` pushed and now merged with main, so PR #36's six CI gates can run. ### 2026-08-03 — A4 round r1 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — the prompt inlined the criteria, and the only files that had to be read in full were `storage.ts`, `filenames.ts` and one house PDF route.
- ACTION: Per `prompts/a4-1.md` on `phase/a4-pdfsrv` from `origin/main` @ `fb61b69`, in worktree `../wt-a4`. **`pages/api/pasantias/brochure.ts` + `pages/api/pasantias/ficha.ts`** are each a doc comment plus one call to a shared factory, **`lib/pasantias/pdf/serve.ts`** (`createPasantiasPdfHandler({document, version, filename, generate})`): GET-only via `handleMethodNotAllowed`; `downloadFile('pasantias/<name>-<VERSION>.pdf')` first, so D-05's manual override is the default path rather than a special case; on a throw (miss or unreachable bucket, indistinguishable and treated the same) it calls A3's generator, `uploadFile`s best-effort, and serves either way — **an upload failure logs and still returns 200 with the generated bytes**. Headers: `application/pdf`, `inline; filename="…"; filename*=UTF-8''…`, `public, max-age=3600`, `Content-Length`. Rate limit `RATE_LIMITS.readonly` per D-04's dampening-only wording. The factory imports **no cohort module**, which is what keeps `cohort-commercial.ts` out of the ficha route's import graph. **[C1]** `pages/api/pasantias/brochure.ts` registered in `ALLOWED_COMMERCIAL_IMPORTERS` (plus the new test file, which imports the commercial constants to pin the brochure cache key against the real value instead of a regex), and a new test states the rule directly: the brochure route matches the commercial-import regex, the ficha route does not. **[C2]** `isRfc5987SafeFilename` is now RFC 5987 §3.2.1 `attr-char` (`/^[A-Za-z0-9!#$&+\-.^_\`|~]+$/`) instead of printable-ASCII-minus-a-denylist — the old form accepted SPACE, a bare `%`, `'` and `*`, all of which break an `ext-value`; both filename constants stay valid, and `encodeRfc5987Filename` (per-UTF-8-byte) + `buildContentDisposition` moved in beside it.
- COMMITS: `ec5a0bb`
- TESTS: `npx vitest run __tests__/api/pasantias-pdf.test.ts` → 43/43. `npx vitest run lib/pasantias/__tests__/pdf.test.ts __tests__/lib/pasantias-cohort.test.ts __tests__/scripts/check-price-leak.test.ts` → 84/84. `npm run type-check` clean; `npm run lint` clean; `npm test` → 254 files / 4036 tests passed; `npm run build` success (both routes in `pages-manifest.json`); `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → OK, 266 files scanned, no commercial data.
- FINDINGS RAISED: **(should-fix, PM/owner-owned)** cross-environment cache poisoning is reachable and pinning the CTA origin does not close it — the `propuestas` bucket lives on the one shared Supabase instance and `.env.local` sets `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL=http://localhost:3000`, so a developer hitting the route locally on a cold cache uploads a PDF printing `localhost:3000/pasantias` to the path production reads. [A1] specifies an unconditional upload on miss, so this was not worked around. Options: gate the upload on `VERCEL_ENV === 'production'`, or accept it and make "warm the cache from production first" an operational step. Worth deciding before A6a publishes the link.
- DECISIONS: (1) **Open question answered — the CTA URL is pinned to the configured origin, not the request**: both generators are called without `options.req`, because the bytes are cached in a bucket every deployment shares and their printed URL must not depend on which deployment missed the cache first; `lib/utils/app-url.ts` already refuses the client-controlled `Host` in production, so passing `req` could only change preview/dev behaviour, and in the wrong direction. (2) **One file beyond the prompt's scope list** — `lib/pasantias/pdf/serve.ts`; the alternative was ~60 duplicated lines across two routes that must behave identically forever. (3) **`export const config = { maxDuration: 60 }`** on both routes, matching `generate-propuesta.ts`: unasked-for, but the platform default is what would turn the first cold-cache request after a deploy into a timeout. ### 2026-08-04 — A4 round r3 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — a one-finding remediation with the diagnosis already written by Sol; nearly all of the round was spent waiting on gates under extreme machine contention (three parallel sessions building at once, load average peaking at 47; `tsc --noEmit` took **72 minutes**, the full suite **45**). - ACTION: Per `prompts/a4-3.md` on `phase/a4-pdfsrv`, worktree `../wt-a4r3` detached at `5e5e722` (the branch is held by `../wt-a4-review`), `.env.local` copied in, `node_modules` reinstalled (the recurring evaporated-modules pattern). Closes REVIEW-A4.md **[B1]**. **`lib/propuestas/storage.ts`** — `uploadFile` gains an optional 4th argument `{ upsert }` defaulting to `true`, so every existing caller is byte-identical (the pre-existing `{ contentType, upsert: true }` assertion still passes unmodified, and `pages/api/licitaciones/[id]/generate-propuesta.ts` — the only other production caller — is untouched). New exported `StorageObjectExistsError` plus a private `isAlreadyExistsError()` that matches **the SDK's error shape, never a message substring**: `@supabase/storage-js` declares `StorageError` with `status`/`statusCode` and `StorageApiError` with `code`, documented as "the service-specific error code … such as `NoSuchKey`, `AccessDenied` or `ResourceAlreadyExists`. Use this to branch on the specific error rather than parsing the message." The classifier accepts `code === 'ResourceAlreadyExists'` **or** `status === 409` **or** `statusCode === '409'` — three fields because the storage API has carried the status under either name across versions and `code` is the newer addition; a conflict is **terminal, not retried** (the object exists; asking twice cannot change that), and the branch is guarded by `!upsert` so upserting callers keep the old semantics exactly. **`lib/pasantias/pdf/serve.ts`** — the cache fill now passes `{ upsert: false }`; the catch branches on `instanceof StorageObjectExistsError` → one `console.info` and a 200 with the freshly generated bytes, everything else → r2's unchanged `console.error` degrade path. A doc-comment paragraph ("WHAT PROTECTS IT") states create-only as the **mechanism** that makes the path a publishing surface rather than a cache with a convention attached: reading first is not enough because a read can fail while the object is healthy, and a manual upload can land between our miss and our write. **Why the shared helper was touched at all** (the one file outside the prompt's stated scope): Sol's finding names `lib/propuestas/storage.ts:28-34` as half of the defect and `upsert: true` is hard-coded there; the alternatives were duplicating bucket name + retry policy inside `serve.ts`, or flipping the shared default and changing the licitaciones caller. Opt-in option, unchanged default, one call site changed.
- COMMITS: on `phase/a4-pdfsrv` — (this commit) create-only cache fills + storage option + tests + review-request r3 section + this entry.
- TESTS: `npx vitest run __tests__/api/pasantias-pdf.test.ts lib/propuestas/__tests__/storage.test.ts` → **84/84** (route 71, was 61; storage 13, was 8). **Falsification check: with `{ upsert: false }` removed and nothing else changed, the route suite fails 8 of 71** — the new cases detect the exact defect. Gates: `npm run type-check` exit 0 · `npm run lint` exit 0 (`--max-warnings=0`) · `npm test` → **253/254 files, 4069/4070 tests**, the single failure a **30 s timeout** in `lib/propuestas/__tests__/generator.test.ts › produces a valid PDF buffer for preparacion type` — a PDF render starved by the contention above, which mocks `uploadFile` and is untouched by this diff; **re-run in isolation it passes 7/7 in 15.8 s** · `npm run build` exit 0, 156/156 static pages, both `.next/server/pages/api/pasantias/{brochure,ficha}.js` present and both routes in `pages-manifest.json` · `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → OK, 266 files scanned, no commercial data. `test:db` and `e2e` not run: no SQL, no UI (same as r1/r2).
- FINDINGS RAISED: none.
- DECISIONS: (1) **The conflicting request serves its own generated bytes rather than re-reading the stored object.** Sol wrote "preferably re-read it for the current response"; the r3 prompt specified serving the generated bytes with an info log, and its test list (b) requires exactly that. Followed the prompt — flagged here because it means one request can serve generated bytes while the designed file sits at the path; every later request serves the designed file, and a re-read is one `downloadFile` call away if the PM prefers it. (2) **Optional 4th parameter on the shared helper** rather than a Pasantías-specific uploader — rationale above. (3) **Three-way OR in the conflict classifier** — broader than a single-field match; a bucket returning 409 for something other than a duplicate would be logged as benign, but the storage API has no such case. (4) **No retry on conflict** while genuine failures keep the helper's two attempts. (5) **Branch NOT reconciled with `main`** — out of the prompt's scope; see below.

It imports `cohort-public` only, so price-freedom is structural (D-01/D-02) — the leak guard is unaffected and still green at 266 files. The brochure URL is resolved **without** the request: `Host` is attacker-controlled and the link outlives the request. **`pages/api/pasantias/lead.ts`** — POST-only, 5/min, honeypot before validation, then `createServiceRoleClient()` for every read and write (the live table gives `anon` nothing and `authenticated` SELECT only). Reads `(email_normalized, cohort)`; inserts or updates; asks `canTransitionLead(existing.status, 'new')` before re-opening a dismissed lead; 23505 is re-read and handled as the duplicate path; new, duplicate and honeypot all end in the identical `200 {success:true}`. Consent evidence is re-stamped every submission; marketing columns are written all-or-nothing and an existing `true` is left **unwritten** rather than overwritten when the box is unchecked.

- TESTS: `npx vitest run __tests__/lib/pasantias-leads.test.ts __tests__/api/pasantias-lead.test.ts` → **75 passed** (42 + 33). Gates: `npm run type-check` exit 0 · `npm run lint` exit 0 (`--max-warnings=0`) · `npm test` → **255 files / 4067 tests passed** (+75) · `npm run build` exit 0, route registered as `ƒ /api/pasantias/lead` · `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → OK, 266 files. `test:db` not run (zero SQL, per the prompt); e2e belongs to A6b.

A6b needs it accepted here, or the PM should retire it. **(2) Two of the caps are inferred.** [A2] names six (80/80/140/140/40/1000) for what reads as first/last/email/institution/phone/message; `role_title` and `utm_*` are unnamed, and unbounded strings on a public endpoint are a real hole, so both are capped at 140. If the intended reading was `role_title = 40` rather than `phone = 40`, that is one constant in `LEAD_FIELD_LIMITS`. **(3) `strict: false` bites the union.** This repo compiles non-strict, so TypeScript will not narrow a `{ok:true}|{ok:false}` union on the false branch; `LeadValidationResult` therefore declares both fields on both members. Worth knowing before the next phase writes a result union and loses twenty minutes to it.
- DECISIONS: (1) **`canTransitionLead(s, s)` is false** — a no-op is not a transition, and the route depends on it: a `new` lead's update simply omits the `status` key. A8's triage UI should be written knowing this. (2) **"Never clears an opt-in" is an absent key, not a preserved value** — `marketingColumns()` returns `{}` in that case, which is correct for a partial update but dies silently if a later phase switches this call to an upsert. (3) **The auto-reply dedup is read-then-write with no lock** — two submissions racing inside the same second both see a null `brochure_sent_at` and both send. Bounded (one duplicate courtesy mail per 24 h window) and cheap; a conditional update seemed out of proportion. (4) **Consent evidence is overwritten, not appended** — one row per `(email, cohort)` leaves nowhere to keep a history; if an audit wants the first acceptance as well as the latest, that is a schema question.

### 2026-08-03 — A5 round r2 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — single-concern round; the r1 code was read in full before any edit and never came close to crowding.
- ACTION: Added the `source_path` write path the PM's [A2] optional-field list had omitted, hardened as untrusted input. `sanitizeSourcePath()` in `lib/pasantias/leads.ts` accepts only a same-site relative path (leading `/`, second char neither `/` nor `\`, no whitespace/C0/DEL, ≤200 chars after trim) and returns null for everything else; `validateLeadSubmission` carries the result on `ValidatedLead.sourcePath` and can never emit an error for it. The route writes `source_path` unconditionally on INSERT (null included) and, via `sourcePathColumns()`, only when a fresh accepted path arrived on UPDATE — so a resubmission from a page that sends nothing never erases the original attribution. Round-r2 section appended to `docs/planning/reviews/fase-a5-review-request.md`.
- COMMITS: (this commit)
- TESTS: `npx vitest run __tests__/lib/pasantias-leads.test.ts __tests__/api/pasantias-lead.test.ts` → **92/92 PASS** (75 in r1, +17 new; every r1 test unchanged and green). Gates: `npm run type-check` clean; `npm run lint` clean (0 warnings); `npm test` → **255 files / 4084 tests PASS**; `npm run build` PASS. `test:db` not run — no schema change. e2e belongs to A6b.
- FINDINGS RAISED: none.
- DECISIONS: **(1) Invalid values are DROPPED to null, never rejected with a 400** — the prompt delegated this choice. Nobody types `sourcePath`, so a `fields.sourcePath` error would reach a form with nowhere to render it, and the asymmetry is decisive: dropping costs one lead's attribution, rejecting costs the lead. Over-cap is dropped for the same reason, which keeps one rule for the column instead of a 400 for length and a null for shape; that is why the field has no `LEAD_VALIDATION_MESSAGES` entry. **(2) The check runs on the RAW string, not `normalizeText` output** — `normalizeText` collapses `\s+` to a single space, which would turn `/pasantias\r\nX-Injected: 1` into a valid-looking `/pasantias X-Injected: 1` and store it. Every other optional field still goes through `normalizeText`; this one deliberately does not. **(3) Allowlist, not sanitiser** — the function returns the value verbatim or null, never a rewritten string, so a stored path is byte-identical to what the browser reported and A6b must still escape it on render like any other stored string. **(4) Requiring the leading `/` is the scheme defence** — no `javascript:`/`data:`/`https:` denylist to keep current; `//host` and `/\host` are refused separately because browsers resolve both against another origin.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none. (`source_path` is the only new write path to this table; a future third writer would not inherit the insert/update asymmetry for free — noted in the review request, not a defect.)
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM verification, then Sol. Both r1 carry-forwards still stand and were not touched this round: consent evidence is overwritten not appended (schema question), and the auto-reply dedup is read-then-write (bounded to one courtesy mail per 24 h). No merge without Brent's explicit go.

### 2026-08-04 — A5 round r3 — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — the whole round lives in three files that were read in full before any edit; the only pressure came from waiting on gates, not from reading.
- ACTION: Per `prompts/a5-3.md` (recovered by the PM in `c91a640` — the earlier `b06ac0c` announced the sharpened prompt in its message but committed only LEDGER + REVIEW; I started from the stale `phase/a4-pdfsrv` copy and switched when the real one landed). Both BLOCKING findings were **the same defect shape in one file** — a decision computed in application memory from a row read earlier, then written unconditionally — so both are closed by removing the dependency on the stale read, not by narrowing a window. **(1) Consent:** `marketingColumns()` now takes `mode: 'insert' | 'update'` instead of the selected row. On `update` the three columns are absent unless this submission is itself the opt-in — not false, not NULL; `insert` keeps the complete false tuple, which is D-12's safe non-assertion on a row with no prior state. `marketing_opt_in` was also dropped from `EXISTING_COLUMNS`, so the value is never fetched: a future edit would have to deliberately add the column back before it could reintroduce this. **(2) Dedup:** claim-then-act. A conditional UPDATE takes the 24 h window and PostgreSQL evaluates the predicate against the row it has locked. `shouldSendAutoReply` is **deleted** and replaced by `autoReplyClaimCutoff(now)` — an exported predicate over `brochure_sent_at` is the check-then-act shape itself, and leaving it invites a future caller to reintroduce the race. A claim that *errors* counts as lost: "we could not tell" must not read as "go ahead". **(3) [S1] from REVIEW-A5,** taken because the blocker rewrote the same seven lines: `buildBrochureUrl()` no longer shares a `try` with `sendLeadNotification`, and is resolved *before* the claim, so a configuration throw costs neither FNE's notification nor the window. **(4) SHOULD-FIX:** `sanitizeSourcePath` no longer trims — chose to correct the code rather than the prose, because the verbatim contract is the one worth having; surrounding whitespace is now a refusal and the cap applies to the raw length.
- COMMITS: `1e69136` on `phase/a5-lead-api`, from `c91a640`.
- TESTS: targeted `npx vitest run __tests__/lib/pasantias-leads.test.ts __tests__/lib/pasantias-emails.test.ts __tests__/api/pasantias-lead.test.ts` → **106 passed** (92 in r2, +14). Gates, all from this commit per REVIEW-A5 [S2]: `npm run type-check` exit 0 · `npm run lint` exit 0 (`--max-warnings=0`) · `npm test` → **222 files / 3888 tests PASS** (Sol measured 221/3874 at `b65dfa8`; +1 file and +14 tests is exactly this round) · `npm run build` exit 0, `ƒ /api/pasantias/lead` registered, 156 static pages · `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` OK over 263 files. `test:db` not run — zero SQL this round. Both blockers mutation-checked: reverting `marketingColumns` to consult the snapshot, and ignoring the claim result, each turn the relevant new tests red.
- FINDINGS RAISED: **`.or()` on UPDATE would have shipped the same bug this round was fixing.** My first implementation claimed the window with one `or=(brochure_sent_at.is.null,brochure_sent_at.lt.<cutoff>)` filter — the obvious expression, and it passed every test. PostgREST **rejects `or` filters on UPDATE** for non-PK columns while accepting them on SELECT, so no fake or unit test can catch it; this repo already paid for that once (`lib/bots/store.ts:claimSessionTransition`, 2026-06-12 stranded-session incident). The claim is now two single-predicate statements — `.is(col,null)` then `.lt(col,cutoff)` — which is correctness-equivalent because whichever wins sets the column to `now`, satisfying neither predicate. **Any future phase writing a conditional UPDATE through PostgREST needs this**: B3/B4's `claim_campaign_sends` and `complete_campaign_if_done` are exactly this shape, and D-06 already routes them through SECURITY DEFINER SQL, which sidesteps it — worth keeping that way rather than expressing them as PostgREST filters.
- DECISIONS: **(1) A failed send does NOT always release the claim.** `sendSoft` now classifies: `not_configured` (no key — the request was never built), `rejected` (Resend answered and refused — nothing queued), `unknown` (the call threw — a timeout can follow an accepted request). `canReleaseAutoReplyClaim` releases on the first two and keeps on the third. [A7] is an upper bound on messages, so a second copy is the one failure this endpoint refuses to risk — that rules out releasing on a throw. But holding on a missing key would be worse than useless: nothing was sent, nobody can be mailed twice, and the row would carry a `brochure_sent_at` that is simply a lie for 24 h — including on every local environment, where `.env.local` has no `RESEND_API_KEY`. **(2) The release is guarded on the stamp still being ours** (`.eq('brochure_sent_at', nowIso)`), so it can never stomp a claim taken in between, and it restores the prior value — null or already-expired, either way claimable. **(3) The test fake now EVALUATES the claim** against real per-row `brochure_sent_at` state instead of replaying a scripted result, and has no `.or()` at all. That is what makes the two-simultaneous-submissions test mean anything — the second request loses because the first moved the column — and it makes a regression to the `or` form fail loudly rather than silently pass. **(4) Trim removed rather than the contract reworded** — nobody types this field, a browser never sends surrounding whitespace, so its presence says hand-crafted; refusing beats cleaning, and trimming is precisely what would launder a wrapping CR/LF into an accepted path.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none. r1/r2 carry-forwards still stand untouched: consent evidence is overwritten not appended (schema question), and `source_path`'s insert/update asymmetry would not be inherited by a third writer.

The mailed brochure target `/api/pasantias/brochure` still exists only on the unmerged A4 branch.

### 2026-08-03 — A5 round r3 — Fable (PM verification)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: n/a
- ACTION: Verified: 106/106 re-run by PM. Both blockers closed at the shape level, not patched: `marketingColumns(mode)` cannot consult a stale snapshot because `marketing_opt_in` was **removed from the fetched columns entirely** — the racy input no longer exists; and `shouldSendAutoReply` was **deleted** rather than left exported, because a predicate over `brochure_sent_at` IS the check-then-act shape the round removes. Both fixes mutation-checked. The rollback answer is better than the question deserved: a failed send releases the claim only when nothing can have left the process (`not_configured`, `rejected`) and **keeps** it when the transport threw, since the provider may already hold the message — release guarded on the stamp still being ours. SHOULD-FIX resolved by fixing the code rather than the prose (trim removed, 11 whitespace shapes refused, values byte-identical). Deviations accepted: fixing Sol's [S1] inside the same seven lines the blocker rewrote, and adding a test file for the 24h arithmetic nothing pinned.
- **FINDING OF THE DAY, forward-binding on Track B:** the executor's first claim used a single PostgREST `or=(brochure_sent_at.is.null,...)` filter on an UPDATE. **It passed every test.** PostgREST *rejects* `or` filters on UPDATE for non-PK columns while accepting them on SELECT — so the form is invisible to fakes and fails only in production, and **this repo has already paid for it once** (`lib/bots/store.ts:claimSessionTransition`, the 2026-06-12 stranded-session incident). The claim is now two single-predicate statements. `claim_campaign_sends` and `complete_campaign_if_done` are the same shape; D-06 already routes them through SECURITY DEFINER SQL, which sidesteps this entirely — **B4a/B4b must keep them there and must not express them as PostgREST filters.**

- TESTS: 106/106 (PM re-run)
- FINDINGS RAISED: PostgREST or-on-UPDATE trap → binding constraint on B4a/B4b
- DECISIONS: deviations + rollback rule ratified

- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable
