Each mutation ran inside an outer transaction the suite's own ROLLBACK undid; nothing persisted on the shared local stack.
- FINDINGS RAISED: none blocking. **Two ambiguities resolved by judgement, both flagged for the reviewer:** (1) PLAN B3 [A1] says "`email_campaign_sends` FK `ON DELETE RESTRICT`" without naming which FK — v2 said *contact* FK, v1 said both CASCADE. Applied RESTRICT to **both**, reasoning from D-04's unqualified "no code path deletes send history"; behaviourally free only if B9a's draft-only delete never meets a draft campaign holding send rows, which D-07 guarantees (queue leaves `draft` exactly when it queued nothing). (2) The identity CHECK additionally requires `unsubscribe_token IS NOT NULL` on the live shape — a strengthening D-08 needs, but B6's import must let the column default fire rather than passing explicit NULL.

(c) `email_webhook_events.detail` is `jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'` — the rule that it stay PII-free is documented in table/column comments; it is enforceable only inside `process_webhook_event`, i.e. B4b [A3].

### 2026-08-03 — B3 r2 (proof hardening: ACL tuple pins + per-field identity CHECK) — Opus (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — one `.sql` test file plus an evidence driver; the costs were `npm ci` in a fresh worktree and repeated `supabase db reset` cycles forced by a shared local stack.
- ACTION: Closed REVIEW-B3.md [B1] and [B2]. **The migration is byte-identical to r1** (`git diff supabase/migrations/` empty) — no mutation showed the schema wrong, so per the prompt nothing in the DDL was touched. [B1]: the ACL pins now compare the whole `aclexplode` tuple. Four drivers × 5 tables replace two: anon = `(none)`, authenticated = `SELECT` rendered as `PRIVILEGE [WITH GRANT OPTION]`, **PUBLIC (grantee 0) = `(none)`**, and **no grantable entry anywhere on the table, any grantee** — the last one is version-independent by construction (it enumerates whatever the ACL holds instead of naming privileges a server may not define), so it needs no PG15/PG17 split; the existing MAINTAIN guard and TRUNCATE/service_role probes are untouched. [B2]: every term of the two-shape identity CHECK is now killed on its own line. r1's cases each violated a term in *both* arms, so dropping any one term left the other arm rejecting the row and the test green; each new case violates exactly one term. 142 → **164 asserts**; suite 313 → **335**.
- COMMITS: (this commit)
- TESTS: `supabase db reset && npm run test:db` → **Files=8, Tests=335, Result: PASS** (suite plan `1..164`). Fail-on-mutant driver committed at `docs/plan/evidence/b3/mutation-driver-r2.sh`, verbatim output at `mutation-probe-r2.txt`: **18 mutants, 18 killed**, baseline and restored schema green — Sol's two mutations verbatim (M-A1 WITH GRANT OPTION, M-A2 REFERENCES to PUBLIC), plus SELECT-to-PUBLIC on the tombstones, a grantable `service_role` INSERT, and one probe per CHECK term (M-C1…M-C14). The driver captures `pg_get_constraintdef()` + the full ACL before touching anything and re-compares after every restore, aborting on any drift. Four JS gates (unaffected by a change confined to one `.sql` test file; run anyway): type-check 0, lint 0 `--max-warnings=0`, `npm test` **253 files / 3992 tests passed**, build 0 `✓ Compiled successfully`.
- FINDINGS RAISED: **two proof gaps the fail-on-mutant driver found that prose reasoning had ruled out — both now closed inside this round, neither a schema defect.** (1) I argued in a code comment that the live arm's `email IS NOT NULL` and `email_normalized IS NOT NULL` were redundant beside `email_normalized = lower(btrim(email))` and therefore *equivalent mutants*, unkillable by any fixture. Wrong: **a CHECK rejects only on FALSE — a NULL result ADMITS the row.** Dropping either guard turns the arm from false to NULL for a half-identified contact, so the mutant commits rows the shipped constraint rejects. A 120-row disagreement census (committed in the driver) found 3 and 5 disagreeing shapes and printed them; two new live cases kill both. (2) `anonymized_at IS NOT NULL` could not be pinned by an otherwise-empty row because `unsubscribe_token` DEFAULTs to `gen_random_uuid()`, so `unsubscribe_token IS NULL` rejected first and the arm's marker term went unproven — M-C1 survived the first full driver run for exactly this reason; the fixture now passes an explicit NULL token. **Process finding (not code):** the local Supabase stack is shared between worktrees and a `supabase db reset` run from a branch without this migration dropped the five tables mid-run, twice, voiding two driver runs. The driver now records the applied migration head at start and re-checks it at the end so an interfered-with run cannot be published as evidence — but that detects, it does not prevent; serialising DB gate runs across concurrent sessions remains a process rule with no mechanism behind it.
- DECISIONS: (1) Grantability is asserted globally per table (`no ACL entry anywhere is grantable`) in addition to the per-role tuple pin, so the same widening applied to `service_role` or the owner also fails — proven by M-A4. (2) The mutation driver is committed as a script rather than narrated, so the runs are reproducible and the restore is machine-checked. (3) `email_normalized IS NOT NULL` and `email IS NOT NULL` are pinned by the two half-identified live shapes rather than declared redundant — see FINDINGS.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM verification, then Sol round 2 (of max 2). Branch `phase/b3-email-db` pushed; no merge without Brent's explicit go. Post-merge owner step unchanged: the migration must be APPLIED to the production database.

### 2026-08-04 — Sol round 3 FAIL: r4 lost four shapes and disclosed one; r5 staged — Fable (PM)

- **REGRESSIONS introduced by r4** (r3 fires, r4 misses): `€2\n500` newline · `€2 500` **narrow NBSP U+202F** · `€2 500` **thin space U+2009** · `€2.500e3`. Cause: the retired `[.,\s]?` separator matched every Unicode space `\s` covers; r4's `NUMBER_TOKEN` grammar enumerates a narrower set. **Four shapes lost, one disclosed** — the executor tested its new cases and assumed the old ones, and I accepted the round without diffing against the prior version.
- **PRE-EXISTING, both versions identical**: fullwidth digits `€２５００` (miss) · doubled space `€2  500` (miss) · `€25000e-1` (miss) · `€2500e-1`=250 (fires, false alarm) · `€2.500e0`=2.5 (fires, false alarm). I repeated the executor's claim without running it against `ca8e024` — the same class of miss I have been penalising in others. The trade bought nothing; r5 restores newline.

That threat model goes into the file as a comment and the limits get pinned as explicit expected-misses, so they are recorded rather than rediscovered every round.

### 2026-08-04 — Sol round 4 FAIL: r5 lost four shapes; my "zero lost" was wrong; r6 staged — Fable (PM)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable
- ACTION: Sol found a fourth real defect in this file, in the round I recommended merging without review. **Confirmed by me against `ca8e024`: `€2 500\n7`, `€2 500 7`, `€1 000\n45` and `€2<U+202F>500 7` all fire on the pre-regression scanner and miss on `2158c44`.** The r5 claim of zero coverage lost — which I measured myself and put in this ledger — is false.
- Cause: `whitespaceRuns` is all-or-nothing. It takes the maximal token, finds the fused reading harmless, then splits on *every* whitespace run, so a protected amount sitting in a whitespace-grouped **subsequence** (`2 500` inside `2 500\n7`) is never read. Vector (b) — a pasted price with a digit landing nearby — not the accepted doubled-space limit.
- **MY VERIFICATION WAS THE PROBLEM, not just the executor's.** My 47-case harness tested `€2 500` alone and `€2500\n7` alone and never their composition. Sol predicted this exact failure before finding it — "both could share a blind spot" — and was right. The executor's corpus had the same hole: it covers punctuation-grouped-plus-gap and bare-integer-plus-gap but not the two combined. **Enumerating hand-picked examples has now missed a composition three rounds running.** r6 therefore requires the differential to be *generated* by crossing spellings with adjacent-context shapes and run against `ca8e024`'s scanner, not listed.
- **PROCESS: my recommendation to merge without Sol's final say was wrong, and Brent overrode it.** I argued the corpus was worth more than another review; the corpus was authored by the executor whose work it validates, which is the same self-referential defect Sol raised as B2 in round 2 and which I had accepted as a serious finding then. Sol is now **4 for 4** on this file — four reviews, four real defects, each in something I had verified and passed. On this file my verification is not a substitute for it, and the record should say so plainly.

The executor also proved it the harder way, mutating the scanner to `.slice(0, 1)`: 104 cases flagged by the id oracle, 108 by the occurrence oracle, **4 caught only by occurrences**.
- The corpus now **derives** the separator set (`for code 0..0xffff` with `WHITESPACE_COUNT = 25` asserted beside it) rather than listing it, so the axis that was wrong three times by three authors — including mine at 21 — can no longer be wrong by omission. Suppression was made **unconditional** (window evaluated) rather than merely pinned, which is stronger than the prompt asked. Sol's corrected figures reproduce exactly: 154 losses at `2158c44` (not 112) and 231 id changes (not 182).
- Gates re-run by me at the r7 head: **6098 unit tests**, production build, clean scan (263 files), **14/14 e2e**.
- **The executor's reported e2e red was environmental and it diagnosed it correctly**: a copied `.env.local` has no `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`, and since r3's B5 change `getAppBaseUrl` throws instead of falling back to `Host`, so `/pasantias` 500s and the specs look like a regression. Adding the key turns all 14 green — confirmed here. **This bit me too**: the checkout I rebuilt for Brent at `~/dev/fne-lms` inherited the same gap, now fixed in both it and the worktree. Worth remembering that the B5 fix converted a silent fallback into a loud failure *by design*, and a red e2e after copying an env file is the expected shape of that, not a defect.
- COMMITS: `99825c31` (executor) · this commit (PM)
- TESTS: as above, all independently re-run
- FINDINGS RAISED: none
- DECISIONS: the fourth-file deviation (evidence doc) accepted — PLAN.md's META requires evidence under `docs/plan/evidence/<phase-id>/` and r4–r6 did the same. The `€120 €70` build-level mutant accepted as the right choice: no verdict regressed this round, so what a build proves that a unit test cannot is that `main()` still reports one finding per figure.
- **NO MERGE RECOMMENDATION — awaiting Sol**, per the commitment made at r6 and kept at r7.
- **STOPPING RULE, stated in advance so it is not decided after seeing the verdict:** if Sol returns further *evidence-completeness* findings against a guard it has already confirmed functionally sound, that is the point to ship. The corpus can keep improving on `main` without holding a finished page hostage. A finding of an actual leak path, or an actual coverage loss, is a different matter and would earn another round.

### 2026-08-05 — Sol round 6: two findings, both reporting-completeness; stopping rule applies — Fable (PM)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable
- ACTION: Sol returned REQUEST CHANGES with two blocking findings, and both are real. **B1**: the corpus asserts the `(check, amount)` multiset separately from the ordered spans, so a mutant that *rotates* labels between spans passes all 1918 scoped tests — `€120 €70` could attribute 120 to the 70 span undetected. **B2**: suppression runs before marker association, so in `€1\n560 EUR` the independently-marked `560 EUR` is discarded and only `priced-amount:1560` is reported.
- **Classified against the rule recorded at r7, before this verdict was seen.** The rule turns on one question: can either finding let a price reach a client bundle undetected, or cause a coverage loss? **I tested B2 directly across 16 constructed container/inner shapes including Sol's verbatim case: zero go silent.** Every input that fired at `ca8e024` still fires at HEAD; B2 takes a report from two findings to one and never changes the exit code, so the build still fails and the price still does not ship. B1 is a test-precision gap against a scanner Sol has now measured at **0 losses, 0 expectation disagreements, clean production scan (263 files)**.
- Both are therefore **reporting completeness, not leak paths**. The rule says ship.
- **Stating the counter-argument honestly, because this is me invoking my own rule:** Sol is now 6 for 6 on this file, its fixes are precisely specified, and B2 is a genuine behavioural defect rather than merely a test gap. Against that: the guard is strictly better than the pre-regression baseline; the page has been finished and byte-identical for six rounds; A6b — the form that actually lets someone sign up — is blocked behind this; and the October cohort is selling now. Both findings can be fixed on `main` without holding a finished page.
- **RECOMMENDATION: merge A6a, and open a follow-up round for B1 + B2 on `main` immediately.** Not "later" — the follow-up is worth doing, and B2's marker-aware suppression in particular should not sit. But it does not belong in front of the phase.
- COMMITS: this commit
- TESTS: PM silence-check across 16 container/inner shapes (0 silenced); Sol's independent gates all green at `99825c31` — 6098 unit, build, clean scan, 0 losses on both the 1133-case and 1818-case corpora
- FINDINGS RAISED: none
- DECISIONS: applying the r7 stopping rule as written. Recorded that the classification test was run *before* the recommendation was formed, not after.
- BACKLOG ADDED: **A6a-followup** — B1 atomic `(check, amount, start, end)` assertions with the rotation mutant failing; B2 marker-aware suppression with `€1\n560 EUR` expecting both occurrences.

- TESTS: `npm run type-check` exit 0 · `npm run lint` exit 0 (`--max-warnings=0`) · `npm test` → **257 files / 4240 tests PASS** (4216 at r2; +19 leak-guard cases, +5 oracle cases) · `npm run build` exit 0 (`ƒ /pasantias`, 7.79 kB) · `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → **OK over 267 files with the wider alternation — no window needed narrowing** · `CI=1 npx playwright test $(node scripts/ci/e2e-mandatory.mjs --list)` → 14 passed (smoke 2, pasantias 6, footer 6); `ci-fixture.spec.ts` (4 failed / 2 not run) needs T2's seeded local stack and this `.env.local` points at the remote project — unrunnable here, unrelated to this round. **Three mutation proofs, each run:** (1) `CURRENCY` back to `(?:€|EUR)` → **9** named leak-guard tests red (`2500 EUROS` survives it, because `EUR` is a prefix of `EUROS` — the accident that made the old guard look like it worked); (2) Sol's three data mutations applied one at a time → the school-table oracle red twice, the expert-table oracle red once; (3) both colour classes and both footer `h2`s reverted, rebuilt → axe red with `serious · color-contrast · 15 node(s)` naming `pasantias-expert-2/3` and `pasantias-objective-*` (Sol's fifteen, exactly), the whole-document heading test red, and the footer spec red on `/programas`, `/brand-preview`, `/pasantias` while still **green** on `/`, `/nosotros`, `/equipo`. All restored, all green.
- FINDINGS RAISED: **(1) Three pages carry pre-existing `heading-order` violations of their own** — `/nosotros` (h1 → h3 "Transformar"), `/programas` (h1 → h3 "AULA GENERATIVA"), `/brand-preview` (h2 "4. Cards" → h4). The Footer change regresses none of them and cannot: the footer's headings come last in DOM order, so raising their level can only remove a jump. Out of scope, so the new footer spec asserts only the transition into and inside the footer rather than adopting or hiding three unrelated pages' debt. **(2) The h4→h2 defect was invisible on half the pages that had it** — `/`, `/nosotros` and `/equipo` end their bodies on an `h3`, so `h3 → h4` was a legal step and only pages ending on `h2` failed. A shared-component a11y defect that presents on a subset is the shape worth remembering. **(3) `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is inlined at build time, which makes CI's step order load-bearing** — I tried to prove B5's throw by stripping the origin vars from `.env.local` and restarting `next start`, and got HTTP 200: the value was already baked into the server bundle. So the `.env.local` step must stay ahead of `npm run build` in `ci.yml`, or the variable is written into a file nothing reads. The workflow now says so in a comment. **(4) axe rates `heading-order` *moderate*** — a serious/critical filter, which is what B4 asks for, does not catch B3's defect. The two e2e checks are not redundant and neither may be dropped for the other.
- DECISIONS: **(1) `h2`, not `h3`, for the Footer.** `h3` looks like the conservative choice and is the wrong one: on a page whose last body heading is an `h1`, an `h3` footer is itself a two-level jump. `h2` is legal after any level, and going *down* a level never violates the rule. **(2) A new token rather than repointing `brand_accent_hover`.** That colour is a hover/surface value used across the repo and is correct there; the defect is using it as small text on white. `brand_accent_text` names the constraint it satisfies, and both existing amber tokens are untouched. **(3) The `aria-hidden` bullet was changed too**, although axe never flagged it — one page with two different ambers on adjacent elements is an inconsistency with no argument behind it. **(4) `Visita en Barcelona`, not silence.** It is still a claim, just a far weaker one, and it rests on A-5's own "están fuera de Barcelona" implying the other five are inside. Called out in the review request as the one place the PM may still find the inference too generous — the honest fallback is to print nothing on those cards. **(5) The word-form boundary is on letters, not word characters.** `2500EUR` still matches (a leak shape); `Europa` and `eurocentrismo` do not. **(6) The new footer spec went on `MANDATORY_SPECS`** — CI runs only that list, so a guard that is not on it is a guard that never runs; `/noticias` and `/noticias/[slug]` were checked by hand instead of automated, because their headings come from a client-side read and CI starts its stack with `realtime` excluded.
- BACKLOG ADDED: heading-order debt on `/nosotros`, `/programas`, `/brand-preview` (finding 1) — three unrelated marketing pages, PM's call whether it is worth a micro-round.
- NOT DONE: nothing from the prompt. S2 was rejected by the PM and not acted on; no single flaky test appeared across the four full-suite runs (4240/4240 each time), so there is no name to report.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM verification (the four page PNGs under `evidence/a6a/` and the three re-rendered PDF pages under `evidence/a3/` are the inspection), then Sol round 2. Content-pack §6 still contradicts Appendix A-6 on three people — still PM-owned, still open since r2. A6b still replaces the `#programa` panel; A7a still owns the site links. No merge without Brent's explicit go.

### 2026-08-04 — A6a round r4 — Claude Code (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — one script, one test file, and the prompt named the exact function; the cost was wall-clock (four production builds for the mutant proofs), not context.
- ACTION: Per `prompts/a6a-4.md` on `phase/a6a-page` from `ca8e024`. Sol's one remaining blocking residue: `wholeAmountPattern`'s tail was `(?:,\d{2})?`, a comma and nothing else, so `2500.00 euros`, `2,500.00 euros`, `EUR 2,500.00` and `euros 2500.00` all walked past the guard — and Sol proved the first of them reached `.next/static/chunks/pages/pasantias-*.js` on a real build while the script exited 0. **I took the prompt's preferred approach: normalisation, not a fourth alternation.** `scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` now tokenises a written number **maximally** and normalises it to a canonical integer; `PRICE_AMOUNT_PATTERNS`/`BAND_AMOUNT_PATTERNS`/`RETIRED_SHORT_AMOUNT_PATTERNS` become `PRICE_AMOUNTS`/`BAND_AMOUNTS`/`RETIRED_SHORT_AMOUNTS` holding **values** (`'2500'`) instead of spellings, and `2500`, `2.500`, `2,500`, `2 500`, `2500,00`, `2500.00`, `2,500.00`, `2.500,00` and the minifier's `2.5e3` all collapse to `2500`. The exponential spellings `2\.5e3` and `1e3` left the lists as separate entries because normalisation subsumes them. `GAP`/`BAND_GAP` become character counts (unchanged at 120 and 12) and proximity is a binary search over the file's currency markers rather than a `CURRENCY GAP amount|amount GAP CURRENCY` alternation; a file with no currency marker skips tokenisation entirely, so the rewrite does not slow the scan.
- COMMITS: (this commit) on `phase/a6a-page`, base `ca8e024`.
- TESTS: `npm run type-check` exit 0 · `npm run lint` exit 0 (`--max-warnings=0`) · `npm test` → **257 files / 4261 tests PASS** (4240 at r3; +21, all in the new leak-guard block) · `npm run build` exit 0 · `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → OK over **267** files, same count as r3 · `CI=1 npx playwright test tests/e2e/pasantias-page.spec.ts tests/e2e/footer-heading-order.spec.ts tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts` → **14 passed**. **Production-build mutant proof, twice, in `docs/plan/evidence/a6a/leak-guard-r4.md`:** `<p>Programa: 2500.00 euros por persona</p>` and `<p>Programa: EUR 2,500.00 por persona</p>` injected as visible page copy and built; each build scanned by **both** the r3 scanner (`git show ca8e024:scripts/check-price-leak.mjs`, run from outside the tree so it reads the same `.next/static`) and the r4 scanner. r3 → `OK — scanned 267 file(s)`, EXIT=0. r4 → `FAIL … 1 match(es)`, EXIT=1, naming the chunk and byte offset. Page restored byte-for-byte, rebuilt, guard green on the same 267 files.
- FINDINGS RAISED: none new. The residue itself is the finding and it is closed.
- DECISIONS: **(1) Maximal tokenisation replaces the trailing `(?![.,]\d)` rather than widening it.** The prompt flagged that clause as a trap — it is the r1 S1 false-positive control that keeps `€12.500` and `€2.5000` quiet, and it is the same clause that rejected a dot decimal. It is not loosened; it is made unnecessary. `€12.500` now tokenises as the single number `12.500`, so there is no `500` left over to compare against a protected amount, and `€1.200,70` is `1.200,70`, so there is no `70`. Every r1 S1 control is still pinned and still green, for a structural reason instead of a lookaround. **(2) `2.5000` is rejected by grammar, not by boundary.** `splitNumber` accepts four shapes — plain, plain with ≤2 decimals, grouped in threes by one consistent separator, grouped plus a *different* decimal separator — and four digits after a dot is none of them, so it is not an amount at all. **(3) Newline and tab are deliberately not separators.** The token's separator class is `.`, `,`, space and NBSP. `\s` would let `€70\n120` fuse into `70120` and hide both band figures; the cost is that `€2\n500` no longer fires, which the old `[.,\s]?` caught. Minified bundles are single-line and no convention writes a thousands separator as a newline. Same-line spaces keep the fusion risk in theory (`€70 120`) and that is the accepted price of still catching `€2 500` — documented on `NUMBER_TOKEN`. **(4) Cents are dropped, not compared**, matching the old `(?:,\d{2})?`: `€2.500,00` and `€2.500,50` are both the protected fee quoted with cents. **(5) The exponent is capped at two digits** — uncapped, `1e999999` would ask `shiftDecimalPoint` for a gigabyte of zero padding. **(6) Two negative controls are verbatim live repo code** (`lib/currency-service.ts`'s `EUR: 1050`, `lib/expenseReportExport.ts`'s `"1.234,50" for EUR`), the second of which is an amount written in exactly the dot-grouped/comma-decimal shape this round taught the guard to read — the guard must read it and stay silent.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none.
- NOT DONE: nothing from the prompt. Everything Sol left out of scope stayed out — B2–B5, S1, S3, the pre-existing heading-order debt on `/nosotros`/`/programas`/`/brand-preview`, and `ci-fixture.spec.ts` (still needs T2's seeded local stack; this worktree's `.env.local` points at the remote project). No evidence PNGs re-rendered: `pages/pasantias.tsx` is byte-identical to `ca8e024`.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM verification, then Sol round 2 closure. Content-pack §6 still contradicts Appendix A-6 on three people — PM-owned, open since r2. A6b still replaces the `#programa` panel; A7a still owns the site links. No merge without Brent's explicit go.

### 2026-08-04 — A6a round r5 — Claude Code (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — one script and one test file, and the differential harness answered questions that would otherwise have cost reading; the cost was wall-clock (three production builds), not context.
- ACTION: Per `prompts/a6a-5.md` on `phase/a6a-page` from `3ccdd96`. **I built the side-by-side harness before touching the code, and it found more than the prompt's table did: r4 lost seventeen shapes, not four.** The narrowing from `\s` to `[  ]` dropped **every whitespace character except the two it kept** — tab, newline, CR, vertical tab, form feed, U+1680, U+2000–U+200A, U+2028, U+2029, U+202F, U+205F, U+3000, U+FEFF (fifteen) — plus `€2.500e3` and `€2.500 000`. **(1)** The class is `\s` again, and is written as `\s` rather than as an enumeration, because an enumeration is the thing that silently falls behind. **(2) `canonicalAmount` became `canonicalAmounts`, returning every value a token can denote.** An exponent makes the mantissa's `.` ambiguous: in `2.500e3` it is a decimal point (2.5 × 10³ = 2500), in `2.500e0` a thousands separator (2500 × 10⁰). r4 committed to the grouped reading and lost the first; committing to the decimal reading would lose the second. A guard has no reason to choose, so it checks both and fires if either is protected. **(3) `whitespaceRuns` is the condition on widening the class at all** — with `\n` back in, `€70\n120` and `€2500\n7` would fuse into the innocuous `70120`/`25007`, which is exactly the masking r4's comment warned about. When the fused reading is not itself a protected amount, the figures either side of the whitespace are read in their own right; when it is, it is reported once. That also closes `€2.500 000`, which r4 already masked across the ordinary space it kept. **(4)** The threat model is now written into the script — vector (a) the minifier, which always emits the shortest form, and vector (b) a human pasting a price, which is where NBSP and narrow/thin spaces come from — and every deliberate limit is pinned in the corpus rather than left to be rediscovered.
- COMMITS: `cfd5adc` on `phase/a6a-page`, base `3ccdd96`.
- TESTS: `npm run type-check` exit 0 · `npm run lint` exit 0 (`--max-warnings=0`) · `npm test` → **257 files / 4311 tests PASS** (4261 at r4; +50, all in the leak-guard file, which went 81 → 131) · `npm run build` exit 0 · `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → OK over **267** files in 0.12 s, same count and same speed as r4 · `CI=1 npx playwright test tests/e2e/pasantias-page.spec.ts tests/e2e/footer-heading-order.spec.ts tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts` → **14 passed**. **Production-build mutant proof (`docs/plan/evidence/a6a/leak-guard-r5.md` §3):** `Solicita el programa completo por €2 500` — the narrow NBSP r4 lost — injected as visible page copy and built; r5 → `FAIL … 1 match(es)`, EXIT=1, naming chunk and byte offset; **r4's own scanner on the same `.next/static` → EXIT=0**. Page restored byte-identical to `ca8e024`, rebuilt, guard green on the same 267 files. **Corpus bite proved twice (§2):** flipping one expected verdict fails by name, and re-applying r4's narrowing to the live scanner produces 15 named failures, one per lost separator.
- FINDINGS RAISED: **(1) The regression was four times larger than the review found, and the reason generalises.** Sol raised "scientific notation and Unicode"; the PM's side-by-side found four shapes; a corpus found seventeen. Enumerated character classes fail this way — nobody diffs a class against `\s` by eye. Any future guard in this repo that narrows a built-in class should carry a corpus that pins the old class's members, or it will lose members silently. **(2) Maximal tokenisation trades one failure mode for another, and the trade is not free.** It is the right false-positive control — it is what keeps `€12.500` and `€2.5000` quiet without lookarounds — but every character added to the separator class widens the window in which two unrelated numbers fuse into one harmless value and hide both. r4 saw this and responded by narrowing the class, which cost fifteen spellings and did not even close the hole (the space was still in). `whitespaceRuns` is the shape that closes it: read the fused value **and** the parts, prefer the fused one when it is itself protected.
- DECISIONS: **(1) `€25000e-1`, fullwidth digits and `€2  500` stay misses; `€2500e-1` and `€2.500e0` stay over-firings** — the prompt's §3 scoping, taken as written after checking it. All five are pinned as explicit expected verdicts in the corpus with the reason attached, so they read as decisions rather than gaps. **(2) I did not add negative-exponent support**, though "shift the decimal in both directions" could be read that way. Adding it would have made `€2500e-1` stop firing — one of the two over-firings §3 says not to fix — and `€25000e-1` is longer than `2500`, so no minifier emits it. I read the clause as the two readings of an ambiguous mantissa, which is what `canonicalAmounts` implements; if the PM meant signed exponents, that is a one-line change and this is the flag for it. **(3) `whitespaceRuns` splits on whitespace only.** Splitting on `.`/`,` too would decompose `€1.200,70` into an unrelated `70` beside a euro sign — the exact crying-wolf case Sol's r1 S1 raised — so `.` and `,` groups stay intact. **(4) The fused reading wins when it is protected**, so `€1 560` reports one finding and not two (`1560` and the `560` inside it); this preserves the r1 B2 invariant that a real €1.560 leak is one match.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none.
- NOT DONE: nothing from the prompt. `pages/pasantias.tsx` and `lib/pasantias/cohort-public.ts` verified byte-identical to `ca8e024` before and after the mutant proof. r4's carry-forwards stand untouched: the heading-order debt on `/nosotros`/`/programas`/`/brand-preview`, content-pack §6 vs Appendix A-6 on three people (PM-owned, open since r2), and `ci-fixture.spec.ts` (still needs T2's seeded local stack). `docs/planning/reviews/fase-a6a-review-request.md` still describes r4's scanner — phase-close work, and out of this round's one-file scope.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM verification, then Sol round 2 closure. A6b still replaces the `#programa` panel; A7a still owns the site links. No merge without Brent's explicit go.

### 2026-08-05 — A6a round r6 — Claude Code (executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — one script, one test file, one new corpus module; the differential harness answered by measurement what would otherwise have cost reading. The cost was wall-clock (two production builds), not context.
- ACTION: Per `prompts/a6a-6.md` on `phase/a6a-page` from `2158c44`. **§1 the segmentation.** `whitespaceRuns` was all-or-nothing: for `€2 500\n7` it checked the fused reading (25007, harmless) and then every single piece (`2`, `500`, `7`, all harmless), and never checked `2 500` — the grouped reading a reader sees. `candidateReadings` replaces it: every contiguous run of whitespace-separated pieces is a candidate, sliced out of the original text so the separators it carries are the ones that were written, and only **maximal** protected readings are kept. That is r5's "the fused reading *is* the finding" rule generalised — the whole token is just the longest candidate — so `€1 560` still reports once rather than also reporting the retired `560` inside it. Suppressing a contained reading can never lose a finding: it is never closer to a marker than its container and never carries a wider window. Capped at four pieces, because a protected figure is at most four digits and its grouped spelling at most two pieces; the cap is what keeps the scan linear rather than quadratic in bundle size (measured: 0.07 s vs r5's 0.14 s over the same 267 files). **§2 the corpus, generated.** `__tests__/scripts/price-leak-corpus.mjs` crosses AMOUNT (6) × SPELLING (7–31 by digit count, including all eighteen `\s` characters and every exponential shift) × CONTEXT (8) → **928 generated cases**, each expected verdict derived from the amount *planted*, not from what a scanner returns. **§3 S1:** `shiftDecimalPoint` strips leading zeroes from the shifted integer, so `€0.25e4` denotes 2500 rather than `02500`; only in the exponent path, so a bare `€070`/`€02500` stays silent and is now pinned as such.
- COMMITS: `bd9b89f` (code + corpus) and this entry on `phase/a6a-page`, base `2158c44`.
- TESTS: `npm run type-check` exit 0 · `npm run lint` exit 0 (`--max-warnings=0`) · `npm test` → **257 files / 5225 tests PASS** (4311 at r5; +914, all in the leak-guard file, which went 131 → 1045) · `npm run build` exit 0 · `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → OK over **267** files · `CI=1 npx playwright test tests/e2e/pasantias-page.spec.ts tests/e2e/footer-heading-order.spec.ts tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts` → **14 passed**. **Differential vs `ca8e024` over 964 cases (`docs/plan/evidence/a6a/leak-guard-r6.md` §3): 0 losses, 234 gains, 182 check-id changes, 0 disagreements with the generated expectation.** Every gain and every id change is classified there; 198 of the gains were already r5's, the other 36 are exactly the S1 fix. **Production-build mutant proof (§4)** with `Programa: €2 500 7 cupos.` — the shape r5 lost — injected as visible page copy: on the same `.next/static`, HEAD → `FAIL … 1 match(es)` EXIT=1 naming chunk and byte offset, **`2158c44` → EXIT=0**, `ca8e024` → EXIT=1. Page restored byte-identical to `ca8e024`, rebuilt, guard green on the same 267 files.
- FINDINGS RAISED: **(1) `2158c44` lost 112 cases, not four, and `1560` hid one of them.** The generated differential shows r5 silent on 108 = 2 amounts (`2500`, `1000`) × 18 separators × 3 trailing-digit contexts, plus Sol's 4 named rows. `1560` is absent from that list for a bad reason: on `€1 560 7` r5 *did* fire — on the nested `560`, not on the fee. The finding survived by accident, on the wrong figure, which is why no hand-written spot check caught it. **(2) The failure mode is now three-for-three and it is always the same one.** r4 lost fifteen spellings, r5 lost four, and both rounds reported zero, because each time the corpus tested the dimensions the author had in mind, separately. A list is the author's model of the problem; the bug is always in the part of the product the model does not contain. Generation is the only thing that has caught it — it found the S1 leading-zero class as a by-product, without anyone looking for it. Any guard in this repo whose correctness is "every way X can be written" should cross its dimensions rather than enumerate its cases.
- DECISIONS: **(1) Maximal readings, not all readings.** Reporting every protected subsequence would make `€1 560` two findings, which the prompt's acceptance forbids; suppressing readings contained in a longer protected one reproduces r5's fused-wins rule and is provably lossless (see above). It is why 109 of the 182 id changes exist — `ca8e024` reported the nested `560` as a second finding, and HEAD does not. **(2) Four pieces, not unbounded.** Unbounded windows are quadratic in the number of whitespace-separated pieces inside one token, which in a large JSON asset is unbounded; four is headroom over the two a protected amount can span, and a longer reading denotes ≥5 digits so it can neither be a finding nor suppress one. **(3) The leading-zero strip lives in `shiftDecimalPoint`, not `canonicalAmounts`.** That confines it to tokens carrying an exponent, so shifting-produced zeroes are normalised while a hand-typed `€070` stays silent exactly as the old `(?<!\d)` boundary left it. **(4) The corpus is a separate `.mjs` module, not inline in the test.** The differential harness has to run both revisions' scanners over exactly the cases vitest runs; a plain-ESM module is importable from both, and the alternative — the harness re-deriving the cases — is the circularity this round exists to remove. **(5) The pre-r6 hand-written blocks stay.** They are independent assertions and they encode Sol r1 S1 / r1 B2 / r2 B1 history; deleting them as "subsumed by generation" is exactly the assumed-safe move that cost the last two rounds.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none.
- NOT DONE: nothing from the prompt. `pages/pasantias.tsx` and `lib/pasantias/cohort-public.ts` verified byte-identical to `ca8e024` (`git diff ca8e024 --` empty) before and after the mutant proof. Accepted limits untouched and pinned in `PINNED_LIMITS`: fullwidth and Arabic-Indic digits, doubled space, `€25000e-1`, the two conservative over-firings, bare leading zeroes with no exponent. Carry-forwards stand: heading-order debt on `/nosotros`/`/programas`/`/brand-preview`, content-pack §6 vs Appendix A-6 on three people (PM-owned, open since r2), `ci-fixture.spec.ts` (needs T2's seeded local stack). `docs/planning/reviews/fase-a6a-review-request.md` still describes r4's scanner — phase-close work, out of this round's one-file scope.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: PM verification, then Sol round 2 closure (Sol is 4 for 4 on this file). A6b still replaces the `#programa` panel; A7a still owns the site links. No merge without Brent's explicit go.

Recovered by cloning `origin` to local disk and checking out `phase/a6a-page`, which `git ls-remote` confirmed sits at exactly the prompt's `8382df8`. Everything below was run in that clone.
- ACTION: Per `prompts/a6a-7.md` (found uncommitted in `~/Documents/fne-lms-pm/docs/plan/prompts/`, the only file in that checkout) on `phase/a6a-page` from `8382df8`. **The round changes no verdict the scanner produces.** Sol's r5 review is right that the guard is correct; what was wrong was the evidence, in the two ways the prompt names. **§1 the separator set is derived, not listed.** `WHITESPACE_CODE_POINTS` asks the engine — `for (c = 0; c <= 0xffff; c++) if (/\s/.test(...))` — and `WHITESPACE_COUNT = 25` is asserted beside it, so an engine change surfaces as a named failure instead of thinner coverage. The r6 list held 18, omitting U+2001, U+2002 and U+2004–U+2008; the PM's independent generator held 21. Human-readable names come from a lookup that never decides membership. **§2 the oracle keeps attribution.** `scanText` findings now carry `amount` (the canonical integer that fired) and `start`/`end` (the reading's span); text checks report `amount: null` and their own span so the shape is uniform. The corpus asserts a **multiset of `(check, amount)` pairs** per case rather than a set of ids, and six `ATTRIBUTION_ROWS` assert what each finding's span slices back to. Three contexts feed it: the band **maximum** with its own marker (so `€120 €70` and `€70 €120` are same-id two-figure cases) and marker positions at each check's exact window edge and one character past it, on both sides. **§3 the suppression invariant is evaluated, not assumed.** `maximalReadings` is exported and suppresses a contained reading only when `other.window >= reading.window`. **§4** the live `lib/services/hour-tracking.ts` FX fragment is pinned in `PINNED_LIMITS` as an accepted over-firing, with the reason.
- COMMITS: (this commit) on `phase/a6a-page`, base `8382df8`.
- TESTS: `npm run type-check` exit 0 · `npm run lint` exit 0 (`--max-warnings=0`) · `npm test` → **257 files / 6098 tests PASS** (5225 at r6; +873, all in the leak-guard file, which went 1045 → 1918) · `npm run build` exit 0 · `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → OK over **267** files · `CI=1 npx playwright test tests/e2e/pasantias-page.spec.ts tests/e2e/footer-heading-order.spec.ts tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts` → **14 passed**. **Corrected differential (`docs/plan/evidence/a6a/leak-guard-r7.md` §1.1), over the r6 context set with the separator axis corrected to 25 — 1133 cases: HEAD vs `ca8e024` 0 losses / 234 gains / 231 id changes; `2158c44` vs `ca8e024` 154 losses; 0 disagreements with the generated expectation. Both of Sol's figures reproduce exactly — 154, not r6's 112; 231, not r6's 182.** Over the full r7 corpus (1818 cases, 13 contexts): 0 losses, 312 gains, 284 id changes, 0 disagreements. The 154 is `2 amounts × 25 separators × 3 trailing-digit contexts` = 150 plus Sol's 4 named rows; r6 wrote the same formula with 18 separators and got 112. Every one of the 231 id changes is classified in §1.3 — 176 are HEAD suppressing the `560` nested inside `1 560`, the other 55 are HEAD reading a value `ca8e024`'s literal patterns missed, and no transition drops an id without a wider reading taking its place.
- FINDINGS RAISED: **(1) The prompt's `€560 560` is exactly right, and the proof is now committed.** Mutating `candidateReadings` to `.slice(0, 1)` makes the first planted figure stop firing while the second, with the same check id, survives. Over 1787 cases the id-set oracle flags 104 and the occurrence oracle flags 108: the four it alone catches are the `€560 560` family, where the id-set oracle reads `retired-short-amount` before and after and stays **green**. Verbatim failure in evidence §2.1. **(2) e2e on this phase reads red for an environment reason that looks like a regression.** The three specs first returned 6 failed / 8 passed with `locator('main')` resolving to 0 elements. `.env.local` copied from a working checkout has no `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`; since r3's B5 change `getAppBaseUrl(context.req)` throws rather than falling back, so `getServerSideProps` 500s and `/pasantias` renders nothing. `ci.yml:149` writes that key into the `.env.local` it generates. Adding it and rebuilding (the value is inlined at build time) turns all 14 green. **(3) The primary checkout is dead on this machine** — see CONTEXT PRESSURE. Anyone dispatched to this repo from `~/Documents/fne-lms-working` will hit it before they hit the task.
- DECISIONS: **(1) The `hour-tracking` over-firing is kept, not tightened.** The prompt permits tightening only without weakening the commercial-module mutant proof, and that proof is what the 120-character window exists for: it has to span `currency:"EUR",items:[{id:…,label:…,amount:…}`. Every tightening available here — no newline between amount and marker, a URL exclusion, a comment exclusion — buys silence on this fragment by narrowing exactly that association. So it is pinned verbatim with the reason, which also gives the corpus its first case on the marker-distance dimension. It costs nothing today: the constant does not survive minification, so `.next/static` never carries it. **(2) The invariant is evaluated rather than asserted.** The prompt allowed either. Containment is a theorem about distance (a contained span is never closer to a marker) and says nothing about window width, so the comment claiming a general proof was claiming half of one — true today only because the single nesting the table admits is `1560 ⊃ 560`, window 120 containing window 12. Evaluating the window makes it unconditional. No verdict moves, which the 1818-case differential confirms; four unit tests drive `maximalReadings` directly, including the narrow-container/wide-inner case the amount table cannot produce and behaviour therefore cannot cover. **(3) The r7 contexts are appended, not interleaved.** `CONTEXTS.slice(0, 8)` has to stay the r6 set or the harness cannot reproduce the corpus Sol read — I lost 26 id changes to exactly this before spotting it, and the first run reported 257 instead of 231. **(4) `checksFiring` survives, built on top of the occurrence list.** The hand-written blocks predate the occurrence oracle and pin one figure at a time, where a set is the honest statement; rewriting them would have churned assertions that encode Sol r1 S1 / r1 B2 / r2 B1 history for no gain. The dedup now lives in one place instead of being the default. **(5) The build-level mutant is `€120 €70`, not r5's lost shape.** No verdict regressed, so there is nothing to re-demonstrate; what a build proves that a unit test cannot is that `main()` — which consumes the fields §2 reshaped — still reports one finding per figure. It does: two findings at offsets 20782 and 20787, one check id.

### 2026-08-05 — A6r prompt written from the brand manual — Fable (PM)
- ACTION: Owner supplied `Brand Kit FNE.pdf` v1.0 and asked for a Claude Code prompt: keep the site's menu/header and stay coherent with nuevaeducacion.org, take the design system from the manual, change no information, and use more Barcelona photography. Read all 20 pages and wrote `prompts/a6r-1.md` against it.
- **The manual already contains the canonical treatment for this exact page** (p.14): full-bleed photo, black gradient veil rising from the text edge to 85% and dissolving before the focal point, yellow eyebrow, white Black-weight headline in caps — captioned "PASANTÍAS INTERNACIONALES / APRENDER DONDE SE HACE" over a Barcelona aerial. The prompt makes that the hero and reuses it for section breaks, which is also the answer to "more photos": photography is the only place the brand permits abundant colour, so it is the mechanism for breaking up A6a's wall of white cards.
- **`tailwind.config.js` already matches the manual's palette exactly** — `brand_primary` #0a0a0a, `brand_accent` #fbbf24, `brand_accent_hover` #f59e0b, `brand_accent_light` #fcd34d, `brand_gray_dark` #1f1f1f, `brand_gray_medium` #6b7280. Only the gold gradient (#FDB833→#B47410, 135°) is missing. Prompt says use tokens, never raw hex, and flags that `brand_accent`/`brand_accent_hover` are surface colours at 1.66:1 and 2.14:1 on white — `brand_accent_text` (#b45309, 5.02:1) is the one that may carry text, which is the A6a r3 contrast fix expressed as a brand rule rather than a test.
- **Header coherence resolved without scope creep:** five pages inline their own nav and there is no shared component. The prompt says replicate `index.tsx`'s header exactly and **explicitly forbids extracting it** — that refactor belongs to A7a, which already edits all five files for link rewiring. ### 2026-08-05 — Sol FAIL on A6r: 3 blocking, all verified; r3 staged — Fable (PM)
- ACTION: Sol returned FAIL. Verified all three before writing the round; all three are real.
- **B2 is the serious one and I confirmed its mechanism at source.** The page resolves four photo slots and eight portraits with **37 `existsSync` probes per request** against `public/`. I read the clean build's `.next/server/pages/pasantias.js.nft.json`: **14 traced files, zero under `public/`, zero images**, and `next.config.js` sets no `outputFileTracingIncludes`. Vercel bundles function runtime files from that trace and serves `public/` separately, so the probes can resolve **every photo as missing in production** — a page that looks right here and ships photo-less. `ogImageUrl` then falls back to a file the same probe called missing, which is a broken social preview on a page sold by WhatsApp link.
- **I did not prove the production failure** — it depends on Vercel builder behaviour I cannot exercise locally, and the r3 prompt says so rather than overclaiming. It doesn't change the fix: resolving static-asset availability at build time is right regardless of whether this platform tolerates the probe, and 37 syscalls per request for a value that changes only when a file is added is the wrong shape anyway.
- **B3's root cause is narrower than Sol stated, and it is a brand deviation.** The hero *does* have a veil — `from-brand_primary/[0.45] via .55 to .80` — but it is **45% exactly where the eyebrow sits**, which is why `#FBBF24` at 11px measures ~3.10:1 at 390px against the 4.5:1 it needs. **The FNE brand manual specifies the veil reaches 85% under the text.** So the implementation is weaker than the brand requires and the contrast failure follows from that. r3 therefore asks for the veil to be brought to spec *first*, with Sol's white-eyebrow option (≥5.18:1) as the fallback — fixing the cause rather than recolouring around it, and keeping the yellow eyebrow the design intends. Also notable: axe files photo-backed text as `incomplete`, so the serious/critical gate passing is not evidence here.
- **B1 is the same lesson for the third time.** The hardcoding guard is a hand-maintained string list checked with `includes()`, so it misses numbers (`{COHORT_VISIT_DAY_COUNT}` → `9` passes), derived dates, any newly added module field, and trivial composition (`['Escola','Virolai'].join(' ')`). **The leak scanner's separator list was wrong three times by three authors before it was fixed by deriving instead of listing** — r3 requires the cases be derived recursively from `COHORT_PUBLIC` with an asserted leaf count, paired with a render contract, and proved by mutating a string, a number and a short value.
- COMMITS: this commit (`prompts/a6r-3.md` + this entry)
- FINDINGS RAISED: none beyond Sol's. Its S1 is right — the review request and evidence README both predate the portrait and FAQ commits and describe a page that no longer exists.
- OPEN: dispatch `/exec INSPIRA A6r r3`.

### 2026-08-05 — A6r r3 verified; PM found a false-pass in the new guard; r4 staged — Fable (PM)
- ACTION: Verified r3 (`046ea328`). **B2 closed and confirmed in the build output**: `grep -c existsSync .next/server/pages/pasantias.js` → **0**, with all 14 image paths inlined at module scope from a generated manifest, and og:image omitted rather than guessed when unguaranteed. **B3 closed**: the veil now runs 92% at the base, **85% where the eyebrow sits**, dissolving to 15% — the manual's treatment — measuring **8.72:1 at 390px and 8.55:1 at 1440px** against the 4.5:1 needed, with a pixel-sampling e2e test at both widths so it cannot regress into axe's `incomplete` bucket. The executor kept the yellow eyebrow rather than taking the white fallback, which was the better outcome.
- **PM FINDING — the new render contract has a false-pass mode, proven on this head.** I hardcoded the literal `2,5` in place of `{formatDays(immersionDays)}`; the guard passed **15/15**. I then set **both** immersion schools to `immersionDays: 4` in the module, rebuilt, served and fetched: the page rendered **"2,5 días cada una"**. Module said 4, page said 2,5, guard green.
- **Cause:** the contract mutates one leaf and asserts the render *changed*. Mutating `immersionSchools[0].immersionDays` makes the two schools disagree, `uniformImmersionDays()` returns null, and the clause disappears — the render changed, so the leaf reads as wired. It changed for the wrong reason. **This generalises: any leaf read through a conditional, aggregate, uniqueness check or length comparison can satisfy "changed" by collapsing rather than by rendering the new value.**
- The fix is one assertion: **"the render changed" is wrong; "the render contains the new value" is right.** Leaves that genuinely can't be checked that way — booleans read as flags, `tier` compared not printed, `id` in a testid — must be classified explicitly in the existing `EXPECTED_GAPS` machinery rather than silently falling back to the weaker check. r4 also requires re-deriving afterwards, since some leaves will start failing that were only ever passing vacuously.
- **Resolved the executor's brand finding myself, since I read the manual and it cannot.** It flagged that 85% contradicts the handoff's readme ("at least 40%") and `tokens/colors.css` (`--veil-photo: .4`). **Both numbers are in the manual and govern different things:** p.6 sets **40% as the minimum for the white LOGO over photography**; p.15's canonical treatment sets **85% under TEXT**. The handoff readme conflated them. Its implementation is correct and stays; I appended the correction to the handoff doc so the next reader doesn't re-derive it.

### 2026-08-06 — A6r r5 verified; the executor corrected my B1 verdict and it was right to — Fable (PM)
- SESSION: `SESSION: INSPIRA · A6r · PM`
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable
- ACTION: Verified r5 (`0c4d87bb`, pushed). **The headline result is that my own verification in the previous entry was wrong, and the executor caught it.**
- **I declared B1 closed on evidence that could not support it.** My attack hardcoded *two* counts at once — `Las 7 escuelas` and `Los 13 objetivos` — and I read the resulting failure as proof both were caught. Run apart, both failures came from the objectives half. The schools literal, which is the one Sol actually reported, passed 28/28. **A compound mutation cannot attribute its own failure**, and I have now made that mistake in a phase whose last three rounds were all about tests that lie to themselves.
- The mechanism is structural and I confirmed it at source: `.length` is printed at **two** sites for schools (`pages/pasantias.tsx:618` hero, `:806` section title), and the inherited `publishesCount` compared *totals* — hardcode one site and the other still puts the new count on the page and takes the old one off, so both halves are satisfied.   - schools **title site alone** → fails 2 by name, including "catches a hardcoded count — the seven schools the hero and the section title both print". This is the exact case that passed before the round.
  - schools **hero site alone** → same two failures.
  - schools **both sites** → same two. The counting half catches what the per-site half hands back to it, as documented.
  - **freeDays at one of its two sites** → fails. This one matters most to me: it is the collection the executor did *not* tune against, so the fix generalises rather than answering the reported literal.
  - **immersionSchools**, whose shrink also moves `uniformImmersionDays()` → fails, attributably.
  - **visitSchools** → fails. **objectives** → fails 2.
- **My first attempt at these attacks was itself broken** and reported all-green: I passed the mutation strings to `perl` without exporting them, so the substitution was a no-op against an empty pattern. Caught it because the harness printed no mutated line. Recorded because it is the same failure mode as the finding above — a check that cannot fail proves nothing, and the only defence is making the harness show its work.
- Gates re-run by me on the committed head, not taken from the report: **6156 tests / 262 files**, type-check clean, lint clean, production build clean, leak scan clean (263 files), **16/16 e2e**. Every number matches what it reported. Page byte-identical to `bcfa1b71` — `git diff bcfa1b71 -- pages/pasantias.tsx` is empty.
- **Spot-checked its 27 re-verified reasons at source rather than counting them.** Every one I sampled holds: `COHORT_ID` and `COHORT_DATE_LABEL` genuinely absent from the import block at `:30-46`; `uniformImmersionDays()` at `:75-78` reads only `immersionDays` and never `tier`, so the retraction is correct; the week cards print `startDate`→`endDate` at `:259-261`; `fullDay` at `:855-858` selects between two strings of the page's own copy. The two pinned constants are asserted exactly with `toHaveLength`, not compared loosely.
- Its deviation — changing the mechanism instead of stopping with FINDINGS — was the right call and it declared it. The round's own criterion was "a hardcoded count must fail by name, permanently"; that criterion was not met when it arrived, and the guard file was already this round's scope. The cost is that `printsStaleSize`, the quiet module and `withOneStaleSite` reach Sol unreviewed by anyone but me.
- FINDINGS RAISED — **one, SHOULD-FIX, not blocking.** `printsStaleSize` returns `false` for any collection of fewer than two elements (`:932`), silently falling back to the counting half — the half that just failed. The docstring says no such collection exists today, and that is true, but it is **prose, not an assertion**: nothing fails on the day a one-element collection is added, and a count printed at two sites would then be hardcodable at one of them undetected. This is the exact shape the round just spent itself on — a stated reason with nothing enforcing it. One assertion closes it. Under the stopping rule it does not earn a round: no customer-visible fact can go stale today.

A move, not a rewrite, and not inside a behaviour-changing round. Its judgement that none of the *behaviour* should be cut is right — every clause answers a demonstrated false pass.
- DECISIONS: the stopping rule recorded before r5 stands, but its clock starts now. I wrote it believing B1 was closed; it was not, and this is the round that closes it.

I confirmed the mechanism rather than the symptom: **both full leaves render at other, properly wired sites** — the label at `:740`, the summary at `:757` — so mutating either leaf moves the page, containment is satisfied, and the leaf reads as wired while the fragment sits there going stale. The source scan looks for the complete leaf; the render proof requires only the complete leaf to lose an occurrence. Neither can see a fragment. **This is precisely the multi-site shape r5 fixed for counts, on strings, and nobody generalised it.**

The design is two-week by construction — two cards, two treatments — so a derivation that could print "Tres semanas" beside two cards trades a stale fact for an incoherent one. Both target strings are written into the prompt verbatim so nothing is improvised.
- The guard extension is specified by shape, not by implementation: after mutating a string leaf, no substantial fragment of the old value may survive; the fragment floor must be **derived and asserted** like `MIN_SCANNED_LENGTH`, never tuned until green. Told the executor to expect more than Sol's two and to declare what it cannot fix — *"do not raise the floor until the noise disappears; that is how the hand-maintained list got its holes."*

- Sol's judgement on the 1551-line guard: **keep it, every mechanism answers a demonstrated false pass**, and do the support-module extraction *after* the correctness round because mixing them would make the next review harder. Agreed and left in backlog; the r6 prompt puts it out of scope explicitly.

### 2026-08-06 — Sol FAIL again on one site; I had accepted the declaration that hid it; r7 staged — Fable (PM)

- **The round found a real bug that the floor had been hiding since r5, and this is the part worth carrying forward.** `quietValue` marked **whitespace-delimited tokens**: `value.replace(/\S+/g, …)`. So `'Barcelona.'` became `Barcelona.zzq`, and the word-boundary matcher — which stops at any non-letter — still saw a bare `Barcelona` **at a properly wired site**. Removing the floor is what surfaced it, as a false positive against correct code. The fix marks the *word* rather than the token (`/[\p{L}\p{N}]+/gu` → `Barcelonazzq.`). **The quiet module has been imperfect since r5 and nobody could see it**, because every value it mismarked was either under the floor or never adjacent to punctuation in a way that collided. Its judgement to repair the mechanism rather than declare `Barcelona.zzq` as an expected restatement is right, and it said so in exactly the phase's own terms: the alternative would have been "a declaration that is true of the code and false as a proof".
- **The declarations meet the standard, including one nobody asked for.** The `lodgingArea` reason cites all nine coincidental sites line by line — I opened `:152`, `:518`, `:755`, `:832`, `:945` and every one is accurate — then names the three *wired* sites and states that "Barcelona" typed into any of them makes ten and fails. The five `visitSchools[*].tier` declarations are written **index by index rather than derived from `COHORT_VISIT_SCHOOLS`**, with the reason stating why: a declaration generated from the module is satisfied by whatever the module says. That is the standing rule from the r8 entry, applied by an executor that was never told it.
- Gates re-run by me: **6169 tests / 262 files**, type-check, lint, build, leak scan (263 files), **16/16 e2e**. Guard file alone: 42 tests.
- FINDINGS RAISED: **none.**
- **The cost I demanded be reported, reported and now owned by the owner.** The `lodgingArea` declaration stands at **nine sites — four prose, five per-visit-school labels.** Any copy edit that adds or removes the word "Barcelona", and any change to the visit-school list, moves that count and reds the suite until someone updates the declaration. The failure message names the leaf, the form and both counts, so the fix is mechanical — but it is friction on every future edit to this page, and it is the direct price of closing a hole on a value whose real-world staleness risk is close to zero. Recorded as a fact rather than an argument: Sol's finding was correct on the contract, and the contract is what [A1] says.
- **Size, unchanged as a concern and growing:** the guard is now **2550 lines against a 1235-line page**. The extraction to `__tests__/support/cohort-contract.ts` remains the largest debt this phase leaves behind.

### 2026-08-05 — A6r round r3 — executor (Claude Opus 5)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — the render-contract harness was the only part that needed more than one attempt, and it converged in two runs.
- ACTION: Sol's three blocking findings, plus its should-fix. All four addressed; nothing else in the page was touched.
  - **B1 — the guard is derived now, and paired.** `__tests__/pages/pasantias-hardcoded-cohort.test.ts` no longer carries a hand-maintained list. It walks `COHORT_PUBLIC` recursively for every primitive leaf — **150**, asserted, with `schools` skipped by object identity because it holds the very same objects as `immersionSchools ++ visitSchools` rather than a second set someone would have had to exclude by hand. Two mechanisms with different blind spots: a **source scan** over the string leaves (12-char floor kept, ISO dates exempt), and a **render contract** that renders the page once per leaf with that leaf changed and reports any leaf whose change moves nothing. 150 leaves + baseline, ~300 ms, because the cohort module is mocked as getters over a mutable store — one page import, 151 renders.
  - **B1's proof, which is the part that matters.** I hardcoded three values into the real page and ran the guard. A string (`weeks[0].summary`, pasted verbatim) → the **source scan** names it *and four more* (`immersionSchools[0].name`, `immersionSchools[1].name`, `experts[2].school`, `experts[3].school` — the sentence contains both school names), and the **render contract** names `weeks[0].summary`. A number (`{COHORT_VISIT_DAY_COUNT}` → literal `9`) → **only the render contract** catches it, naming `visitDayCount`. A short value (`{COHORT_LODGING_AREA}` → literal `Barcelona`, nine characters, and one of its three uses is composed into an eyebrow with `COHORT_LABEL`) → **only the render contract**, naming `lodgingArea`. Sol's two named misses, reproduced and then caught.
  - **The mutation rules are derived, not listed.** Strings get every token suffixed — a page restating one word of a claim while rendering the rest would otherwise still look wired. Numbers shift, booleans flip. ISO dates shift the **day**, not the year: the page prints day and month, so a year change renders identically and the contract would have passed vacuously on all eleven date leaves. Fields with two-to-four distinct values at the same field name mutate to another of **their own** values — `tier` is read as `=== 'inmersion'`, so `'visita'` and `'visitazzq'` render the same and all five visit schools' tiers would have read as unwired. That last one only surfaced because the first run reported them; it is in the file as a rule, not as five exclusions.
  - **Documented limits are pinned, not silent.** Six leaves are declared in `EXPECTED_GAPS` with a reason each — `id` and `dateLabel` (the page imports neither; it renders `COHORT_HEADLINE`), all of `visitDays` top-level and per week (the itinerary prints ranges and a count, never the individual dates), and `freeDays[1].date` (the long weekend prints first-to-last, so the middle day's date never appears; its label does and is covered). They are asserted to **stay** gaps, so one that starts rendering fails and must be deleted, and a stale prefix that matches nothing fails too. A new module field belongs to neither list and therefore fails the contract — which is the whole point.
  - **One real restatement fell out of it.** `data-testid="pasantias-week-semana-1"` was `weeks[0].id` written out by hand. Now `` `pasantias-week-${immersionWeek.id}` ``. Rendered value identical, so [A3] holds and `tests/e2e/pasantias-page.spec.ts:116-117` was not touched.
  - **B2 — availability is a build-time fact now.** `scripts/generate-pasantias-image-manifest.mjs` writes `lib/pasantias/image-manifest.ts` (14 images, committed); the page resolves slots and portraits with a `Set` lookup at module scope. Verified in the built output: `grep -c existsSync .next/server/pages/pasantias.js` → **0**, all 14 paths inlined into the server bundle, and the nft trace still lists 14 files with zero images — which no longer matters, because nothing is probed. `__tests__/lib/pasantias-image-manifest.test.ts` re-scans the directory and fails on drift, so a photograph added without `npm run images:manifest` is a red test rather than a silently missing image.
  - **B2's missing-file behaviour, stated exactly.** A slot whose candidates are all absent resolves to `null` and renders nothing at all — unchanged from r2. What changed is the `og:image`: it is the hero, falls back to `bcn-skyline.jpg`, and **if neither is in the manifest the `og:image`, `og:image:alt` and `twitter:image` tags are omitted and `twitter:card` drops from `summary_large_image` to `summary`**. Today both candidates ship, so the tags are present and point at `bcn-skyline.jpg`. `getServerSideProps` is pure again — two absolute URLs and the formatted dates.
  - **B3 — the veil is at 85 % under the text, and the yellow eyebrow stays.** Measured against the **lightest** pixel behind the glyphs (a mean passes while a bright patch of sky makes a word illegible), with the glyphs made transparent and the eyebrow's own box screenshotted, so the sample is the composited photograph *and* veil as the browser painted them: **390 px 2.98:1 → 8.72:1**, **1440 px 2.88:1 → 8.55:1**, against the 4.5:1 that 11 px text needs. Sol's white-eyebrow fallback was not needed. Two e2e tests hold it; the PNG is decoded by handing it back to the page as a data URL, so no dependency was added for it.
  - **B3's stop is anchored in pixels from the bottom, and that is deliberate.** The text block is bottom-aligned, so the eyebrow sits ~440–460 px above the hero's lower edge at both widths, while its *fraction* of the hero runs 0.393 at 390×844 and 0.411 at 1440×900 — and drops to ~0.17 at 1440×600, where any percentage stop tuned to the first two would fail. `480px` from the bottom clears it at all of them. The e2e measures two window sizes, not that third one; it is named in the review request as the untested case.
  - **S1 — both documents refreshed.** `docs/planning/reviews/fase-a6r-review-request.md` rewritten for the branch as it stands: 5 commits, the r3 changes, r3's gate evidence, and the r1-era "scrutinise the `existsSync` probe" and "argue with the length floor" items replaced by what actually wants scrutiny now. `docs/plan/evidence/a6r/README.md` corrected — all eight portraits resolve, the initials tile is no longer exercised — and given a section saying which round rendered which capture. Re-rendered from the production build at 390/1280/1440: `00-full-*` (page-level, carries the new hero), `02-hero-*`, `10-equipo-*`. Nine PNGs; nothing below the hero changed in r3.

- TESTS: `npm run type-check && npm run lint && npm test && npm run build && node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → exit 0. `npm test` → **262 files, 6142 tests, all passing**. `npm run build` → compiled successfully. `check-price-leak` → OK, 263 files, no commercial data. `CI=1 npx playwright test tests/e2e/pasantias-page.spec.ts tests/e2e/footer-heading-order.spec.ts tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts` → **16 passed** (14 before, plus the two contrast tests).
- FINDINGS RAISED (one, should-fix, for the owner):
  - **The 85 % veil contradicts the repo's own brand distillation.** `docs/plan/design/a6r-handoff/design-system-readme.md` says "a black veil of **at least 40 %** when text sits on top" and "black at **40–55 %** as a photo veil"; `tokens/colors.css` carries `--veil-photo: rgba(10,10,10,.4)` labelled "minimum veil under text on photography". The r3 prompt states the manual specifies the veil reaches 85 % under the text. I cannot read the manual itself from here, so I cannot say which is the transcription error. What I can say is that **the two cannot both be satisfied**: a flat veil anywhere in 40–55 % puts `#FBBF24` at 11 px at ~3.3:1, and it needs ~65 % to reach 4.5:1 — so the choice was always between exceeding the stated band and giving up the yellow eyebrow. The shipped page had already exceeded it (r1/r2 ended at 80 %). I implemented the prompt's specification and am raising this rather than quietly picking a side. If the 40–55 % line is the correct one, the fix is Sol's white eyebrow and the veil comes back down.
- DECISIONS:
  - **`UNIFORM_IMMERSION_DAYS` became a function.** It was a module-scope IIFE, so `immersionSchools[*].immersionDays` reached the render through a value computed at import and could not be exercised by the contract. Correct in production — the data is static — but it made two leaves untestable, and a gap declaration would have been excusing a wart rather than removing it. Pure computation, called once per render.

The answer is not a better floor, it is the second mechanism: the `lodgingArea` proof shows the render contract catching a nine-character value the scan cannot see. The test says so in its own header rather than implying completeness.
  - **The manifest lists `reference/` images too.** It mirrors the directory rather than encoding a policy about which photographs are shippable; the resolver only ever looks up exact names, and a manifest that filters is a manifest whose drift test has to filter identically.

### 2026-08-05 — A6r round r4 — executor (Claude Opus 5)
- ACTION: Rewrote the render contract in `__tests__/pages/pasantias-hardcoded-cohort.test.ts` around containment. Scope was that one file; `pages/pasantias.tsx` and the module are untouched.
- **The PM's false-pass reproduced first, on the pre-fix head.** Replaced both `{formatDays(immersionDays)}` sites with the literal `2,5` at `046ea328`: **15/15 passed**. That is the defect, confirmed before changing anything.
- **The fix.** `unwiredLeaves` asserted the render *changed*; it now asserts the render *contains the mutated value*, and that this leaf's copy of the old value has left the page. Matching runs against a **rendered surface** — visible text with each tag replaced by a NUL, plus the content-bearing attributes (`data-testid`, `alt`, `title`, `aria-label`, `content`) — not raw markup, because Tailwind class names are full of exactly the digits and short words the contract has to match on and `mt-9` would make a bare `9` look printed anywhere on the page. Contiguous match first, token-wise second: "1 de octubre" is three ordinary words the page is full of, so token-wise alone would find it in the baseline too; and a claim never appears contiguously because `splitClaim` renders its figure and caption in different elements.
- **Two mutations had to change to make containment mean anything.** Dates now shift to a **day no date in the module uses** (derived — the first free day in 1–28, currently the 1st), because the old `(day % 27) + 1` shift turned 9 October into 10 October, which the page already prints as the first free day: the leaf would have read as wired on its neighbour's output. And **the enum-sibling mutation is deleted**. It existed because `tier` renders identically under a marker, but under containment it was actively harmful: `levels` has four distinct values, so mutating one school's into another's produced a string already on the page and the leaf could not be told from a literal.
- **Re-derivation — 12 leaves moved, and every one of them is now declared rather than silently passing.** Three new lists, each asserted in both directions, so a declaration that goes stale fails here instead of standing as an excuse:
  - **`UNIFORM_LEAVES` (2) — `immersionSchools[0..1].immersionDays`.** Read as an agreement: `uniformImmersionDays()` returns a number only while both schools match, so a single-leaf mutation can only make the clause vanish. Proved with the sibling group moved together, which a page with `2,5` typed into it still fails. Weaker in one stated way — siblings holding the same value are indistinguishable afterwards, so this proves the field is read from the module, not which school it was read from.
  - **`UNPRINTABLE_LEAVES` (4) — `immersionSchools[0..1].tier`, `visitSchools[0].fullDay`, `visitSchools[4].fullDay`.** `tier` is compared (`=== 'inmersion'`) and never shown; a boolean has no printable form at all. Only "the output moved" is available for these, and now it says so.
  - **`EXPECTED_GAPS` +5 — `visitSchools[0..4].tier`.** These move nothing whatsoever: the tier arrays are already split in the module, and a visit school never satisfies the immersion filter before or after a mutation. **They read as wired in r3 only because the enum mutation swapped them to `'inmersion'` and into that filter** — output changed, nothing printed. The same false-pass shape the PM found, on five more leaves.
  - The other 7 that surfaced (`weeks[*].summary`, `*.levels`, `experts[*].school`/`note`, `claims[*]`, `dayStructure[*].description`) were my own mutation strategy misfiring, not page defects; they are `rendered` under the corrected mutations, with no declaration.
- **Proofs, at the page rather than in the harness.** Hardcoded each in `pages/pasantias.tsx`, ran the guard, reverted: (1) `2,5` for both `{formatDays(immersionDays)}` → fails naming `immersionSchools[0].immersionDays: consumed, declared uniform` and `[1]` likewise; (2) `12 de octubre` for `{freeDayDates[freeDayDates.length - 1]}`, a length-indexed read used twice → fails naming `freeDays[2].date`; (3) `400+` for `{figure}`, one token of a value the page takes apart → fails naming all four `claims[*]`. Reverted → 21/21. All three passed r3's contract while hardcoded. The same three are now **permanent cases** in `the contract can fail`, so they cannot regress into a manual demo nobody re-runs.
- **Failures name the leaf.** The honesty assertions report offenders on the *received* side as `path: actual, declared expected`; previously a failure printed a truncated list of the leaves that behaved correctly.
- COMMITS: `fffdaba5`
- TESTS: `npm run type-check && npm run lint && npm test && npm run build && node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → exit 0. `npm test` → **262 files, 6148 tests, all passing** (6142 in r3, +6 from this file: 15 → 21). `npm run build` → compiled successfully. `check-price-leak` → OK, 263 files, no commercial data. `CI=1 npx playwright test tests/e2e/pasantias-page.spec.ts tests/e2e/footer-heading-order.spec.ts tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts` → **16 passed**. No evidence re-render: the page does not change this round.
- FINDINGS RAISED: none new. B2 and B3 were left alone as instructed, and the veil question is closed by the PM's manual reading (40 % is the logo minimum, 85 % the text treatment).
- DECISIONS:
  - **`visitSchools[*].tier` declared a gap rather than made testable.** The alternative was to give the page a reason to print or key off a visit school's tier, which would be inventing a consumer to satisfy a test. The module already hands the page two pre-split arrays; the honest statement is that this field does not reach this page.
  - **Sibling-coherent mutation is a fallback, never the default.** Mutating a whole group up front would let a page that hardcodes week 2's start date pass on week 1's output, since both leaves would carry the same mutated value. It runs only after a single-leaf mutation has failed, and only for leaves declared in `UNIFORM_LEAVES`.
  - **The old-value-must-leave half counts occurrences rather than requiring absence, and ignores the mutation's own handiwork.** Appending the marker to "Barcelona" leaves "Barcelonazzq", which still *contains* the original; a plain substring check would read the mutation as having changed nothing. It requires the count to *drop*, so a value the page prints in more than one place is handled without a per-value exception list.
  - **es-CL forms are written out in the test, not imported from the page.** The page's `formatDays`/`formatDayMonth` are part of what the contract checks; borrowing them would let a page that formats nothing still satisfy it.
- BACKLOG ADDED: none.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: Sol re-review of A6r at `fffdaba5`, then Brent's merge word. Untouched and still open from earlier rounds: the two empty full-bleed photo slots (`barcelona-calle`, `barcelona-tarde`), the mockup's "a day inside a school" FAQ topic with no owner-reviewed answer, `styles/globals.css:1` still pulling Inter from Google Fonts app-wide, and the 1440×600 hero case the contrast e2e does not measure.
