### 2026-08-06 — A6r round 5 — Opus (Executor)
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable — the round was scoped to one test file and one review document, and the page was read rather than written.
- ACTION: Resumed the stranded r5 work in `~/dev/wt-a6r`, owned it as author of record, and found that **it did not close B1**.
- **The inherited work passed the literal Sol reported.** The round's evidence — the PM's and the first executor's — was the pair mutation `Las ${COHORT_SCHOOLS.length} escuelas` → `Las 7 escuelas` **and** `Los ${COHORT_OBJECTIVES.length} objetivos` → `Los 13 objetivos`, run together, failing two tests. Run apart: the objectives half produces **both** failures; the schools half — the one Sol actually reported — **passed 28/28**. I ran all four combinations before concluding.
- **Why, and it is structural rather than an oversight.** `COHORT_SCHOOLS.length` is printed at two sites (`pages/pasantias.tsx:618` hero, `:806` section title). `publishesCount` compares *totals*: with one site hardcoded the other still puts an `8` on the page and still takes a `7` off it, so both of its halves pass. Its own docstring declared the case unresolvable — "the sites are indistinguishable because they print the same number".
- **It is resolvable, and it is now resolved.** `printsStaleSize` shrinks the collection one element past its present size and requires the old size to be **gone** from the page. Read on a *quiet module* (`quietValue`): every string leaf marked, so the module's own prose — `COHORT_CLAIMS` holds "7 escuelas en esta cohorte", `COHORT_HEADLINE` holds "Octubre, 5 al 16" — cannot print a bare number, leaving only the numbers the page computes. One surviving `7` is one literal, however many sites moved around it. Shrinking rather than growing: the objectives list numbers its own items, so a fourteenth objective leaves a `13` on the page for an innocent reason, and shrinking takes the ordinal away with the element.
- Second clause only, never a replacement: a count hardcoded at *every* site is caught by the counting half, which is where it shows up. Evaluated lazily, so a collection that never publishes a size is not shrunk — `weeks` is destructured as `[immersionWeek, visitWeek]` and shrinking it would throw rather than answer.
- **Negative controls, run against `pages/pasantias.tsx` itself and reverted each time.** `Las 7 escuelas` alone: 28/28 before, now **2 failed** naming `schools: unpublished, declared published`. The hero site alone (not previously tried): **2 failed**, same names. Both sites: 2 failed. `Los 13 objetivos`: 2 failed. B2 — `school.tier === 'inmersion'` → `school.immersionDays !== undefined`: **2 failed**, naming 7 tier leaves. Reproduced independently rather than taken from the previous round's report.
- **B2 is closed as delivered.** The five `visitSchools[*].tier` leaves are in `UNPRINTABLE_LEAVES` with a predicate-crossing mutation, and the immersion reason retracts the false claim that `tier` feeds `uniformImmersionDays()`.
- **All 27 declared reasons re-verified against the code**, not only the two that changed — 6 `EXPECTED_GAPS`, 9 `UNPRINTABLE_LEAVES`, 2 `UNIFORM_LEAVES`, 10 `UNCOUNTED_COLLECTIONS`, each against a file and line. Every one holds; the list with its line references is in the executor report. Both pinned constants are asserted against a freshly derived value with `toHaveLength`, which is exact in both directions: `EXPECTED_LEAF_COUNT = 150`, `EXPECTED_COLLECTION_COUNT = 21` (independently recounted: 21 arrays, `schools` contributing its length but no leaves).
- **S1 done.** `docs/planning/reviews/fase-a6r-review-request.md` rewritten from its r3 state to the branch as it stands — 9 commits, 6156 tests, r4's containment correction, r5's two additions, the veil question recorded as settled, and the negative-control table.
- **The page did not change.** `git diff bcfa1b71 -- pages/pasantias.tsx` is empty at the end of the round.
- COMMITS: this commit
- TESTS: `npm run type-check && npm run lint && npm test && npm run build && node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` → exit 0. `npm test` → **262 files, 6156 tests, all passing** (6148 at r4; +8 from this file: 21 → 29). `npm run build` → compiled successfully. `check-price-leak` → OK, 263 files, no commercial data. `CI=1 npx playwright test tests/e2e/pasantias-page.spec.ts tests/e2e/footer-heading-order.spec.ts tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts` → **16 passed**. No evidence re-render: the page does not change this round.
- FINDINGS RAISED:
  - **BLOCKING, and fixed in this round rather than reported open:** B1 was recorded as closed on evidence that did not establish it. The mutation that mattered was never run on its own. Worth more than the fix: two of the last three rounds have had a *verification* fault rather than a code fault, and both times the check was a compound mutation whose halves were not separated.
  - **SHOULD-FIX — the guard is 1551 lines, larger than the 1151-line page it guards.** Asked for my opinion and not for a change, so: it is past the point where it protects more than it costs *to read*, not past the point where it protects more than it costs to run (29 tests, 251 ms). Every line answers a demonstrated false pass, so nothing in it is speculative and I would cut none of the behaviour. What I would move: the mechanism — leaf walk, mutation strategies, collection walk, quiet module, classification — is roughly 700 lines and belongs in `__tests__/support/cohort-contract.ts`, leaving the declarations and the suite, which is the part a reviewer of this phase needs. A move, not a rewrite, and it should not happen in a round that is also changing behaviour.
- DECISIONS:
  - **Fixed B1 rather than reporting FINDINGS and stopping.** The guard file is this round's scope, the round's own prompt requires that a hardcoded count fail by name permanently, and the escape hatch is for a broken plan rather than an incomplete inherited diff I am the author of record for. Recording it so the choice is visible: it cost the round a mechanism the PM has not seen.
  - **The quiet module marks strings and leaves numbers alone.** A number the module holds is a fact the page is entitled to print; marking it would blind the check. If a numeric leaf ever equals a counted collection's size the clause fails loudly rather than passing silently, which is the right way round.
  - **`withOneStaleSite` leaves the baseline untouched.** A literal is invisible while the module still agrees with it — that is what makes it a literal — so it can only show itself in the two renders where the module has moved.
- BACKLOG ADDED: move the contract mechanism out of the test file into `__tests__/support/cohort-contract.ts` (see the SHOULD-FIX above); not in a behaviour-changing round.
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: Sol re-review of A6r at this head, then Brent's merge word. The stopping rule recorded before r5 applies — but note it was written on the understanding that B1 was closed, and it was not; this round is the one that closes it. Untouched and still open: the two empty full-bleed photo slots (`barcelona-calle`, `barcelona-tarde`), `styles/globals.css:1` still pulling Inter from Google Fonts app-wide, the 1440×600 hero case the contrast e2e does not measure, and the missing `prebuild` step for the image manifest. ### 2026-08-06 — A6r r8: containment now asks a value to be gone, not to be rarer — Opus (executor)
- SESSION: `SESSION: INSPIRA · A6r · r8 · EXEC`
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable
- **Reproduced the PM's finding before changing anything.** On the committed guard at `7c5b642a`, hardcoding the FAQ answer's `{freeDayRange}` to `10 al 12 de octubre` leaves the suite **38/38 green**. The hole is `provesRendered`'s `countUnmutated(output, form) < countUnmutated(baseline, form)`: two occurrences becoming one satisfies it, so the finde card's own change pays for the FAQ's literal.
- **The round generalises the shape rather than fixing the value type.** `restatementsOn` asks every leaf, in every form `renderedForms` allows, whether anything of it survives on the **quiet module** — where no sibling and no wired site is left to supply it, which is what makes "gone" a question a correct page can answer. It is the general case of `printsStaleSize` (a collection's size) and `survivingFragments` (part of a string); those two solved the same difficulty twice for two value types.
- Two decisions inside it, both stated in the file. It reads one **site** (a text node) rather than the whole page, because the objectives list prints a bare `10` as an ordinal and a page-wide search cannot tell that from a date. It matches a form **token by token within that site**, because a formatted value never reaches the page contiguously — `10 al 12 de octubre` contains neither `10 de octubre` nor `12 de octubre` as a run.
- `provesRendered` keeps `<`. Requiring zero there is wrong on its own terms — El Puig and Les Vinyes share `'Infantil, primaria y ESO'`, so a correct page fails it — and the comment above it, which claimed the stronger rule for four rounds, now says what the line does and points at the rule that asks the strong question where the confounder is gone.
- **`quietValue` now moves numbers too** (`NUMBER_SHIFT`, fractional so a moved number lands on no size the page counts). Quieting was the counting rules' scaffolding and left numbers alone deliberately; this rule needs the module to hold none of its own values. `printsStaleSize`, `weekCountSurface` and the fragment rule all read a quiet surface and all stayed green.
- **Seven leaves surfaced under the strict rule on a correct page, and six are excluded by an inherited floor, not a tuned one.** `isRestatable` reuses `MIN_SCANNED_LENGTH` and `isScannable`'s own reason — below it a string is as likely to be ordinary Spanish as a planted fact, and that function names *Barcelona* and *visita* as its two examples. Those are precisely the six: `lodgingArea: "Barcelona"` against "Por qué Barcelona" (`:755`), "fuera de Barcelona" (`:945`), "recorrer Barcelona" (`:518`); and five `visitSchools[*].tier: "visita"` against "Escuelas de visita" (`:921`) and `{COHORT_VISIT_SCHOOLS.length} escuelas de visita` (`:846`). Dates are exempt from the floor at any length as they are in `isScannable`; numbers are checked at any length. The limit is written down as the rule's limit.
- **The seventh is the only declaration, and it is declared by site *count*.** `visitDayCount: "9"` survives because the contact block prints `+56 9 4162 3577` (`WHATSAPP_DISPLAY`, `:126`, rendered `:1226`) — a bare 9 between two spaces. `EXPECTED_RESTATEMENTS` records **one** site, so a `9` typed into the hero strip (`:705`) or `buildMetaDescription` (`:152`) makes two and fails. Site *text* was rejected as the anchor: these surfaces are quieted module prose and move with `MUTATION_DAY`.

### 2026-08-06 — A6r r9: the floor came out, and it was hiding a second defect — Opus (executor)
- SESSION: `SESSION: INSPIRA · A6r · r9 · EXEC`
- CONTEXT PRESSURE: comfortable
- BRANCH: `phase/a6r-design`, base `159418eb` (Sol's review commit, which was on the branch and unpushed).
- **B1 closed, and the closure is the one Sol asked for rather than either exit it forbade.** `isRestatable` is gone — deleted, not relaxed — so the restatement rule now reads **every form at every length**. No path-level exemption for `lodgingArea`, no floor moved anywhere: `MIN_SCANNED_LENGTH` still stands at 12 for the source scan and the fragment rule, where the reason is still true.
- **The argument for dropping it, since the round turns on it.** A floor protects a *substring search over source text*, where a short value matches any sentence containing the word and nothing tells the two apart. The restatement rule is not that search — it asks whether a value the module no longer holds is still published, and it counts sites. Ordinary copy sharing the word is a fixed, declarable number; one site more is a literal. The floor's r1 premise (the render contract covers what the scan cannot) is the one r8 disproved for multi-site facts.
- **Removing the floor exposed a second, real defect in the quieting — not a declaration, a bug.** The mutation mark was appended per non-space token, so `Barcelona.` at the end of a module sentence became `Barcelona.zzq` and left `Barcelona` standing as a whole word on a module that held nothing. `dayStructure[2].description` (`cohort-public.ts:344`) ends exactly that way and answered a search for the lodging area. Nothing could see it before, because every form long enough to clear the floor spanned more than one token and so carried a mark. New `markTokens` marks each run of letters and digits instead; it is shared by `quietValue` and `mutateValue`, so the render contract gets the same repair. That is what took the declared count from 10 to 9.
- **NEGATIVE CONTROL — `catches the lodging area hardcoded at one site while the others stay wired`.** It identifies the three wired sites mechanically (render with `COHORT_LODGING_AREA` quieted and nothing else; a site that stops printing the value is one that reads it), asserts there are three, then pins each in turn onto the quiet surface and requires `lodgingArea: "Barcelona" at 10` — the count, not just the leaf. **I also ran Sol's attack for real**, editing the page three times: hardcoding `:508`, `:1087` or `:892` each fails **three tests**, naming `lodgingArea: "Barcelona" at 10`. At `1b405ddb` all three were green.
- **The mechanism is pinned against its own regression.** Putting the floor back reds the new control *and* `declares no restatement that no longer survives` — the declarations no longer typecheck as coverage once the rule stops seeing what they declare. A future silent floor cannot pass.
- **THE REAL NUMBER, which the prompt refused to let me tune: six declarations.** One for `lodgingArea` (`"Barcelona"`, 9 sites) and five for `visitSchools[0..4].tier` (`"visita"`, 2 sites each). `'inmersion'` surfaces nowhere and is declared nowhere. The nine Barcelona sites, each checked against its line: `:152` meta description, `:518` FAQ, `:755` eyebrow "Por qué Barcelona", `:832` finde card, and `:945` once per visit-school card (2 "Día completo — fuera de Barcelona", 3 "Visita en Barcelona"). The two `visita` sites: `:846` and `:921`.
- **WHICH A COPY EDIT DISTURBS: the lodging-area count, and it is the whole cost.** Any new sentence containing "Barcelona" makes it ten and reds the suite until the digit is updated. Worse than a copy edit, the same count moves on a **cohort data** change — the five of its nine sites are one per visit-school card, so a sixth visit school moves it too. The `visita` count of 2 is pure page copy and only a copy edit moves it. The failure is loud and names the leaf, the form and the number; the fix is one digit. I did not tune the mechanism to make this smaller, and it is written into `EXPECTED_RESTATEMENTS`' own doc so it is found before it bites rather than after.
- **No correct construct now fails.** The six above are the complete set surfaced by removing the floor, and each is ordinary page copy that shares a word with a module value — told apart from a restatement by the wired sites still being wired: a declaration is the sites the page prints *without* reading the module, which is exactly what the control's quiet-one-export render measures. The one thing that looked like a coincidence and was not — `Barcelona.zzq` — was fixed rather than declared.
- **The page is unchanged.** `pages/pasantias.tsx` is byte-identical to r7; the stricter rule exposed no restatement in it. No evidence re-rendered, per the prompt.
- FILES: `__tests__/pages/pasantias-hardcoded-cohort.test.ts` (+186/−33, now **2550 lines**, **42 tests**).
- GATES: `npm run type-check` clean · `npm run lint` clean (`--max-warnings=0`) · `npm test` **262 files / 6169 tests** · `npm run build` compiled · `check-price-leak` OK over 263 files · `CI=1 npx playwright test` (pasantias-page, footer-heading-order, smoke) **16 passed**.
- FINDINGS RAISED: none. The prompt's B1 was correct and closable as written; the marking bug was found inside it, not against it.
- NOT DONE: the `__tests__/support/cohort-contract.ts` extraction, still deferred and now 2550 lines against a 1235-line page. The source scan and the fragment rule keep their twelve-character floor by design — out of scope here and, unlike the restatement rule's, argued rather than inherited.

### 2026-08-06 — A6b r1 verified: the form is real, and the one guard collision is not as closed as the report says — Fable (PM)
- SESSION: `SESSION: INSPIRA · A6b · PM`

- **WHAT I RAN MYSELF, not read off the report:** `npm run type-check` (clean) · `npm run lint` (clean, `--max-warnings=0`) · `npm test` **four times** · `npm run build` · `node scripts/check-price-leak.mjs` (OK, 267 files) · `CI=1 npx playwright test` over `pasantias-form`, `pasantias-page` and `footer-heading-order` — **20 passed**, axe green with the form present. I read the whole diff (`git diff 43218fcb..5613df78`), not the summary, and checked [A1]–[A9] against `components/pasantias/LeadForm.tsx` line by line.
- **I ATTACKED THE GUARD RATHER THAN TRUSTING ITS GREEN.** The report's central claim is that the A6r guard did not bite because the copy names no cohort fact — but a guard that passes because it cannot see the new file passes the same way. So I planted `components/pasantias/__pm_probe.tsx` containing "Escola Virolai", "Barcelona" and "9 días", ran the guard, and it went red naming the file and both leaves it hit (`immersionSchools[0].name`, `experts[3].school`). Probe deleted, tree clean. **The guard genuinely covers the directory A6b created.** I also traced the render contract: `publishedSurface` calls `renderToStaticMarkup` on the page component, and `<LeadForm />` renders its form branch under SSR (`submitted` is false), so the form's labels and consent sentences ARE on the surface the restatement counter reads. `EXPECTED_RESTATEMENTS` still reading `lodgingArea` / `'Barcelona'` / `sites: 9` is therefore a real result, not a blind spot. [A6] holds.
- **VERIFIED AGAINST THE CODE, not the prose:** `consent: form.consent === true` (strict boolean, which is what `leads.ts:253` demands and what a bare checkbox's `'on'` would fail) · two checkboxes with no shared state and `marketingOptIn: false` in `INITIAL_FORM`, never flipped anywhere in the file · the processing label split on `PRIVACY_POLICY_LINK_LABEL` at render, with a unit test pinning the label's full text so a botched split cannot pass · `validateLeadSubmission` — the route's own validator — called on the exact body before any fetch, so client and server render the identical sentence · honeypot off-screen, `aria-hidden`, `tabIndex={-1}`, value posted · `id="programa"`, `pasantias-programa`, `aria-labelledby` and every surviving CTA testid intact · no commercial import, no price literal.

**FINDINGS — 0 BLOCKING, 2 SHOULD-FIX, 3 NIT.**

- **S1 (SHOULD-FIX) — the `1000` collision is not closed; it moved.** The executor dropped `maxlength` from the message textarea because `LEAD_FIELD_LIMITS.message` is 1000 and the attribute matched D-02's pattern for the retired €1.000 fee (`/(?<![\d.,])1[.,\s]?000(?![\d])/`, `tests/e2e/pasantias-page.spec.ts`). I confirmed the collision is real by reading the pattern. But the same digit string comes back through `LEAD_VALIDATION_MESSAGES.tooLong(1000)` → **"Máximo 1000 caracteres."**, rendered against the field — and removing the attribute is precisely what makes that path reachable, because a person can now type past the cap. Their own unit test at `PasantiasLeadForm.test.tsx:246` asserts that string into the DOM. So the change traded a static occurrence that could never be a price for a dynamic one that also can never be a price, and the report presents it as resolved. **The honest reading is that this is not an executor error but a plan-level tension: D-02's pattern was written for a retired price and collides with an unrelated frozen constant, and no arrangement of this form can keep `1000` off the page while the cap is 1000 and the message says so.** I considered BLOCKING and rejected it — D-02 protects against prices on public surfaces, and a character count is not a price; nothing harmful ships; the D-02 e2e scan runs on a clean load and is green. **Flagged explicitly for Sol**, who has overruled my judgment on this file's guards more than once and should rule on whether the pattern or the copy gives way.
- **S2 (SHOULD-FIX) — a Z2 commit is riding on this branch and is not on `main`.** `43218fcb` "docs(z2): r21 approved" sits between my prompt commit and the executor's first commit: a concurrent ZOOM session committed into this shared checkout at 20:35 while `phase/a6b-form` was checked out. Verified with `git log main..phase/a6b-form`. Docs-only, touches nothing A6b touches, and the executor was right to leave another session's work alone rather than rewrite it. **Not a remediation round** — the resolution is a cherry-pick-or-carry decision that belongs to Brent and the ZOOM PM, not to a fresh executor. It must be settled before A6b merges, and the ZOOM PM needs telling that its r21 entry is not where it thinks it is.
- **N1 (NIT)** — `sourcePath` posts `pathname + search`, so UTM values land in `source_path` as well as the three `utm_*` columns. The A5 sanitizer accepts it. A8's triage surface may prefer the bare pathname; logged for A8, not for here.
- **N2 (NIT)** — the executor's own NOT DONE note says a 429 shows the same generic sentence as a 500. It does not: the handler renders `payload.error || GENERIC_ERROR`, and `lib/rateLimit.ts:175` sends an es-CL rate-limit sentence. The code is better than its note.
- **N3 (NIT, environment)** — one of my four full-suite runs reported `1 failed | 262 passed` with **6180 → 6174 tests and zero assertion failures**, i.e. a suite-level worker crash rather than a real red. Three subsequent runs were `263 passed / 6180 passed`, matching the executor exactly. Not reproduced, no file identified; recorded so it is not discovered a second time as a mystery.
- **THE GATE NEITHER OF US RAN.** The executor could not run the full `MANDATORY_SPECS` list locally — `ci-fixture`, `zoom-*` and `session-*` need the seeded ephemeral Supabase stack CI builds — and neither could I. That is the same limit A6r r7–r9 ran under. CI is the surface that runs the list, and this branch correctly adds `tests/e2e/pasantias-form.spec.ts` to it (`scripts/ci/e2e-mandatory.mjs`), so the new spec will run there. **Nothing about the auth-fixture specs was verified on this machine.**
- VERDICT: **A6b r1 is clean. No BLOCKING finding, so no remediation round. The phase is ready for Codex Sol final review**, with S1 put in front of Sol deliberately rather than filed quietly, and S2 in front of Brent as a merge precondition. The phase is not Done until Sol passes it.

### 2026-08-06 — Sol FAIL on A6b: the finding is real, and underneath it is a class; r2 staged — Fable (PM)
- SESSION: `SESSION: INSPIRA · A6b · PM`
- ROUND: A6b r1 closed FAIL by Codex Sol. Review committed at `docs/plan/reviews/REVIEW-A6B.md`. VERDICT: FAIL, 1 BLOCKING, 1 SHOULD-FIX, 0 NITs.
- **SOL'S B1 IS REAL AND I REPRODUCED IT.** An over-limit `utm_*` query value produces `errors.utmSource` from the shared validator, but the form has no control, no error element and no `FOCUS_ORDER` entry for it — so `handleSubmit` returns early and **nothing happens at all**: no request, no message, no focus. A visitor who filled the form correctly loses the lead and nobody learns. Sol reproduced it in a production build with a 141-character `utm_source` (zero POSTs, no focused element); I reproduced the contract half with a throwaway probe — an otherwise-perfect body with a 141-character `utmSource` returns `ok: false` with exactly one error key, `utmSource`. Probe deleted, tree clean.
- **MY MISS, stated plainly.** The r1 prompt's field table gave the 140-character cap and told the executor to capture UTM from the query, and never said what to do when the captured value exceeds the cap. The executor implemented what I specified. This finding belongs to the prompt.
- **UNDERNEATH IT IS A CLASS, and that is what r2 actually fixes.** UTM is not special — the defect is that the component can hold errors it cannot show. Enumerating `LEAD_VALIDATION_MESSAGES` against the rendered controls turns up a second one already reachable: **`cohort`**. It cannot come from the client (the component imports `COHORT_ID`, so it always agrees with itself), but it arrives from the **server** in exactly the scenario the validator's own comment names — a stale cached page from a retired cohort passes client validation against its stale constant, posts, and gets `400 {fields:{cohort:…}}`. The 400 branch does `setErrors(payload.fields)` and focuses nothing. **Same dead form, on the path the plan anticipated.** So r2 owes a structural fix — no error may ever be invisible — plus a test that enumerates every validator key against the rendered controls, so a field added in a future phase cannot reintroduce the class silently. Two special cases would have left the third one waiting.
- **HOW THE UTM FIX IS SETTLED, and it is not my invention:** A5 already faced this question for `source_path` and chose DROP over REJECT, and the LEDGER records the reasoning (A5 r2) — nobody types these fields, so an error against them reaches a form with nowhere to render it; *"losing one lead's attribution is the cheap failure; losing the lead is not."* r2 applies the ratified rule to the three UTM values. **Drop, not truncate** — a truncated campaign name is a wrong attribution recorded as if it were right.
- SOL S1 ACCEPTED, folded into r2: `payload.error || GENERIC_ERROR` on 429/500/503 makes the component's own contract accidental. The payload is now reserved for the 400 field path; every other failure renders `GENERIC_ERROR`.
- **SOL OVERRULED ME ON S1 OF MY OWN REVIEW, AND IT WAS RIGHT TO.** I classified the `1000` collision SHOULD-FIX and invited Sol to call that too comfortable. Sol's ruling is sharper than either position: `Máximo 1000 caracteres.` is **not** a price and does not violate D-02's semantic boundary, so its reachable state is not a code defect at all — but dropping browser-side length enforcement to keep an ambiguous digit string out of clean-load HTML is *"the wrong lasting trade"*, and the guard should learn the distinction before the form restores `maxLength`. That is a plan/guard item, not an executor item, so r2 is explicitly forbidden from touching the textarea. **BACKLOG + owner decision needed** (see below).
- SOL ALSO AUDITED WHAT I ADMITTED I HAD NOT: the client import of `validateLeadSubmission` carries ~2.6 KB minified of unrelated signup/RBAC runtime into the page chunk, against a 14.4 KB `/pasantias` route. No secret or commercial data. Not grounds for rejection; a future extraction of a client-safe validation leaf would be cleaner. Logged, not actioned.
- **BACKLOG / NEEDS AN OWNER DECISION (neither is A6b's to fix):**
  1. **D-02's retired-price regex cannot tell `1000` the character cap from `€1.000` the dead fee.** Sol recommends teaching the guard the distinction and then restoring `maxLength`. Requires a Decision Log entry against a frozen plan — Brent's call.
  2. **A5 is internally inconsistent about undisplayable fields.** `sanitizeSourcePath` drops rather than errors, for reasons the ledger ratifies; the `utm_*` fields error rather than drop, on the same reasoning that should have dropped them. The server can therefore 400 any client over a field no form can render. A6b works around it client-side; the frozen route still has the trap for the next client.
- **S2 IS GETTING WORSE, not holding steady.** The branch now carries **three** foreign commits — `43218fcb`, `3efa18f9`, `e9c146df`, all `docs(z2)` — because a concurrent ZOOM session keeps committing into this shared checkout while `phase/a6b-form` is the checked-out branch. They are docs-only and touch nothing A6b touches, but the ZOOM workstream's approvals for r21–r23 are sitting on an INSPIRA feature branch and are **not on `main`**. The r2 prompt tells the executor to leave them strictly alone. This needs settling operationally, not at merge time.
- ACTION: `prompts/a6b-2.md` committed. Written as a remediation round on good code, not a rebuild: three findings, a small diff, seven named tests, and an explicit ban on the two things an executor would plausibly reach for — editing the frozen A5 validator to fix B1 at its root, and restoring `maxLength` on Sol's reasoning before the owner has ruled.
- COMMITS: this commit (`REVIEW-A6B.md` + `prompts/a6b-2.md` + this entry)
- OPEN AFTER THIS ROUND: executor runs r2; PM verification (re-run the gates, read the diff, and attack the enumeration test the way the guard was attacked in r1 — a class-guard that cannot see a new key is the same false-pass this project has paid for four times); then Sol round 2 of max 2.

### 2026-08-07 — A6b round r2 — Claude Opus 5 (executor)
- SESSION: `SESSION: INSPIRA · A6b · r2 · EXEC`
- ROUND: remediation of Codex Sol's FAIL on r1. Branch `phase/a6b-form`. Three files touched, no frozen file edited, no new file created.
- **[B1] The over-limit UTM value now costs the attribution, never the lead.** `usableUtm()` drops a `utm_*` value whose normalized length exceeds `LEAD_FIELD_LIMITS.utm` as it is read from the query string, exactly as A5's `sanitizeSourcePath` drops an unusable `source_path` and for the identical reason: nobody types these, so an error against one reaches a form with nowhere to render it. Not truncated — the ratified precedent drops rather than mangles, and a cut campaign name is a wrong attribution recorded as if it were right. Each value is judged independently, so one bad parameter cannot cost the other two.
- **[B2][B3] The class is closed, not the two instances.** Field errors now reach state through one function, `applyFieldErrors`, used by the client-validation path and the 400 path alike. Any error key outside `RENDERED_ERROR_FIELDS` also raises the form-level message (`UNRENDERABLE_ERROR`, es-CL, remedy = recargar la página — the real fix for the cached-page-vs-retired-cohort case the validator's own comment anticipates). The enumeration test derives its key list from `LEAD_VALIDATION_MESSAGES` ∪ `LEAD_FIELD_LIMITS` (with `utm` fanned out to its three body keys) and drives each key through a real 400, so a validator key added in a later phase fails this test rather than silently reintroducing the dead form.
- **[S1] Non-400 failures render the component's own sentence.** `payload.error` is gone from the 429/500/503/unparseable/network path; the 400 `fields` payload is the only server-supplied copy the component still shows.
- **I DID NOT TOUCH THE FROZEN FILES, and the server half of B1 is still open.** `lib/pasantias/leads.ts` still 400s on an over-cap UTM value that no form can render — the inconsistency the PM logged as a plan-level item. r2 fixes it only for this client. The next client of that route inherits the trap.
